# SPEC 124a — Step Standard Policy: Appendix (history, plus register rows R-A…R-AI)

**Status:** ACTIVE (it carries no rulings of its own: the §B7 rows are Spec 124 §5 rulings, see Governance below; the status
line exists so `scripts/generate-system-map.mjs`'s `/^\*\*Status:\*\*\s*(.+)$/m` match does not silently
default this file to `'Done'`).

> **Role: authority register.** Part of the Spec 124 policy register: its LIVE REGISTER sections hold §5 rows moved for the byte budget, in force as Spec 124 rulings; every other section is history (Spec 124 R-GV, R-GW). Sibling of `docs/specs/01-pipeline/124_step_standard_policy.md` (Spec 124). Created 2026-10-02 per the operator ruling "Split to 124a": Spec 124 breached its arm (iv) byte budget in `docs/specs/01-pipeline/122_split_manifest.json` (157,811 B against a 156,781 B ceiling with register row R-BG drafted), and the ruling was to split, not to rebaseline `measured_at`. Each section below arrived ONLY through `node scripts/analysis/spec-split-check.mjs --refresh` (Spec 124 R-AA): its heading names the source anchor, its `moves[]` row in the manifest records the content hash, and the source heading survives in Spec 124 as a stub pointing here.

**Governance:** this file decides nothing of its own. Spec 124's §2 rules stay in Spec 124. **Exception, 2026-10-07 (move `M18`, P1-C9):** Appendix §B7 holds Spec 124 §5 register rows R-A…R-AI, moved for Spec 124's byte budget. They are LIVE and in force exactly as if they stood in §5, are cited as `Spec 124 R-x`, and are amended only by the §4 protocol; `spec-split-check` arm (iii) resolves a ruling citation against §5 plus every 124a section headed `LIVE REGISTER`. Every other section below is history: where it conflicts with current Spec 124 text, Spec 124 governs.

## Operating Boundaries

### Target Files
- None — a non-normative appendix with no owned implementation.

### Out-of-Scope Files
- `docs/specs/01-pipeline/124_step_standard_policy.md` — the live policy; its rules are never moved here. Register rows were never moved here until M18 (2026-10-07, rows R-A…R-AI to §B7, LIVE REGISTER); new rows are still added to Spec 124 §5.

### Cross-Spec Dependencies
- **Relies on:** Spec 124 (the source of every section below; R-AA governs how a block arrives).
- **Consumed by:** readers following a Spec 124 stub; `scripts/analysis/spec-split-check.mjs` (arms i, ii and vi verify every move recorded here; arm iii reads the §B7 LIVE REGISTER rows).

## Appendix §B1 — Worked examples from pilots 1–3 (moved from Spec 124 §7) — HISTORICAL, 2026-10-02

Moved from Spec 124 — Operator ruling 'Split to 124a' (2026-10-02): the drafted R-BG register row pushed Spec 124 past its arm (iv) byte ceiling. A dated pilot 1-3 illustration of the §7 ladder, no 'Enforced by' clause; the ladder itself stays in place.

#### Worked examples from pilots 1–3

| Problem | Rung used | Resolution | Ground |
|---|---|---|---|
| The 345-line JS fallback link path (0 of 11 runs since 2026-04-02 took it) was dead weight with a silent swallow, orphaning its `link_massing_grid_degrees` tunable | (a) descriptor, dead compute deleted | `knowingly-retired` (Intent Ledger), replaced by declared `guards.requires` preconditions; the orphaned tunable gets `config.retired: [{name, since, why, ledger: LM-D7}]`, never a silent seed-row deletion | LM-D7, ruling A-8, R-A |
| `link_massing_link_rate_fail_pct` was the bare literal `50`, INTENT-UNKNOWN | (c) admin logic variable | `link_massing_link_rate_fail_pct` (default 50, 0–100, `on_invalid:fail`) + `checks[].limit_from_config` | LM-D5 |
| No tiebreak on equal-distance nearest-building matches — links could flip between runs | (e) compute change, last resort | `ORDER BY` rule fixed in `buildMatchSql`; the rule itself stated in the check's `why`, in `notes.json`, and in the compute header — not left as a bare code change | LM-D13 |
| A killed forced-FULL retraction left `parcel_buildings` at 29,330/520,492 rows; the next run's gate read "unchanged" | (a) descriptor | `recovery.interrupted` (`force_full_on_next_run`\|`none`+why) required on any destructive-retraction write target | R-B |
| A declared logic variable with no `logic_variables` row silently resolved to the seed default, stamped as if operator-set | (d) library change | `resolveConfig` (all steps, not just `link_massing`) issues one extra presence query and THROWs, naming the remedy | LM-D15 |
| A standing non-zero link-quality population (`nearest_footprint_gt_lot_count`) was dispositioned INFO with no gate | (b) declared check, severity corrected | INFO conflicted with Spec 48 §4.9; re-dispositioned WARN + declared retighten condition | R-H, LM-D6/LM-D11 |

## Appendix §B2 — Known concerns per archetype (moved from Spec 124 §9) — HISTORICAL, 2026-10-02

Moved from Spec 124 — Operator ruling 'Split to 124a' (2026-10-02), same move set as M08. Per-pilot archetype caveats (M06/M07 criterion: a pilot-cutover snapshot, not Rules 1-13 policy), no 'Enforced by' clause; the heading stays, so every 'Spec 124 §9' citation still resolves.

## §9. Known concerns per archetype (R-T, 2026-08-29 — lifted from the ~~six~~ **nine (corrected 2026-09-10, measured)** pilots' §R Reflections)

*One row per proven archetype, citing the pilot report it was first measured in. Not a defect list (see `docs/reports/defect-ledger.md` for that) — this is the standing, archetype-level caveat a future pilot of the SAME archetype should read before assuming its predecessor's shape transfers cleanly.*

| Archetype | Pilot | Known concern |
|---|---|---|
| ASSERT | `assert_schema` | Compute never opens `ctx.pool` at all (Rule 2's stated exception) — the shape's thinnest test of the contract; `outputs`/`recovery`/`counters`/`config`/`override` all forced `"none"`, so ASSERT alone cannot exercise `write_discipline`, `recovery.reset`, or tunable-presence checks at all. Four fences moved to descriptor data or a named check at conversion (Rule 1 worked example). |
| INGESTOR | `load_ravines` | The richest archetype (9 members) but only ONE representative converted — class B/4-`finally`/drift+mass-delete-env-override coverage is real, but classes A and C (the OTHER two write mechanics INGESTOR spans, §8.2's coverage caveat) are validated only by the enum port, never by a converted INGESTOR exercising them directly. LR-D9 (a phase-reordering ruling silently retiring a "before X" write-order guarantee, Rule 11's origin incident) was FIRST found here, not in a more write-heavy archetype. **Update 2026-09-27:** class A is now exercised by converted `address_points`/`parcels` and class C by converted `load_centreline` (batch-2 row 3.2 ③) — the enum-port-only caveat is retired for A and C. |
| LINK | `link_massing` | E1's `guard:"none"` is a genuinely correct grandfathered case (an `IS DISTINCT FROM` guard here would skip the exact rows the clear exists to move) — the archetype's own worked example for why `guard:"none"` is sometimes RIGHT, not merely tolerated (Rule 9). Also the archetype that forced R-B's crash-recovery declaration (an interrupted forced-FULL retraction leaving a table at 29,330/520,492 rows with a FALSE `recovery.resume` claim). |
| MATCHER | `link_wsib` | Tier-3 precision required hardening post-cutover (LW-D18, a WF3) after a self-agreeing predicate (`tier3_token_overlap_pass_pct` at 100.00%) coexisted with a 60-row sample measuring as low as 46.7% genuine precision — R-O's "sampled precision/recall, never a link rate" accuracy doctrine originates here, and is the ONE Rule-10-adjacent doctrine every future MATCHER must apply, not just cite. |
| MATERIALIZER | `link_parcel_addresses` | LPA-D1 (`insert_only_no_retraction`, class D, banned-for-new under V7's `no_retraction` rule) is GRANDFATHERED, not fixed — a real, filed, zero-measured-live-exposure known defect the archetype's sole representative carries forward rather than resolving. A future MATERIALIZER pilot (if the estate ever gets a second one) inherits this as an open question, not a closed pattern. |
| BACKFILL | `compute_centroids` | `write.js`'s class-E (`write_once_backfill`) executor (`executeBackfillUpdate`) was newly built THIS pilot (LG-20) — the "zero library growth" hypothesis every pilot since 3 has tested was REFUTED here too, for the third time. Fork-over-share was chosen a THIRD time over a shared phase-runner scaffold (`LG-21`, RATIFIED fork-over-share at the freeze — `e029c37d`, `template-freeze.json`'s `lg21_decision` — see §8 above). Downstream: this archetype's output (`parcels.centroid_lat/lng`) exposes a real, measured, pre-existing consumer defect in `link-parcels.js`'s Tier-3 join (CC-D2/CC-D3, `RT-CC3` — 6,808/17,500 spatial-tier links, 38.9%, would resolve to a different parcel under a containment-based join) that is NOT this archetype's own defect to fix, but is the first pilot where a downstream consumer's join STRATEGY, not just its data freshness, was found to be in question. |
| RECORDER | *(pilot 8, run — corrected 2026-09-03, WF3 cloud-parity FIX 1.6)* | `refresh_snapshot` converted (`converted.json`, 8 entries) via `runRecorderPhase` (LG-26/LG-27 library growth). Was mislabeled "pilot 7, not yet run" — pilot 7 was `link_parcels` (LINK archetype). RECORDER's daily-keyed upsert fit `guarded_upsert` with `guard:"none"` (declared, not invented); see the pilot 8 commit history and `docs/reports/2026-08-31-pilot8-refresh-snapshot-assessment.md` for the worked answer. |
| ENRICHER | `enrich_parcels` (pilot 9, run; cut over commit 9, 2026-09-11) | **Four measured concerns, all invisible locally — every one only surfaced against real cloud scale/heap-state:** **EP-D14** (2026-09-09) — pass-5 D4' recovery walked unconsumed `enrich_parcels_pass3_scope` rows ONE AT A TIME, a per-row full-scan UPDATE with no index leading on `parcel_id`; cloud-measured 439,130 unconsumed rows, one backend active 23 minutes on a single such UPDATE, invisible locally because EP-D10's own prune leaves the local table at 0 rows. **EP-D16** (2026-09-09) — the declared pass-5 statement-timeout bound was applied ONLY inside `flushBatch`'s per-batch transaction, never session-wide on `postClient`; every OTHER statement on that connection (`consumePendingScope`'s reads/writes, the citywide backstop check) ran with NO ceiling at all, invisible locally because a small local table never runs long enough to expose an unbound statement. **EP-D15** (2026-09-09) — the post_commit phase's "heartbeat" advanced only at 4 phase-boundary call sites, never periodically DURING a phase, so a genuinely healthy 30+-minute pass looked identical to a wedged one; invisible locally because a local pass finishes before the gap would ever matter. **EP-D17** (2026-09-10) — the run-end `invariants[]`/`plausibility[]` post checks (and `run-chain.js`'s T1/T4 telemetry) ran as unbounded, unfolded, serial full scans of `parcels`; cost is not a constant but a 150x cliff keyed on heap state (16-30s clean vs 40+ minutes against the 1,346,759 dead tuples the step's OWN pass 4 had just created), invisible locally because a small local table has no bloat to hit. All four were closed as code fixes in the SAME step's own WF3 cycle, not deferred to a future pilot's discovery. |
| ENRICHER *(superseded 2026-09-11 by the row above — retained, never deleted)* | *(pilot 9, not yet run — corrected 2026-09-03; was mislabeled "pilot 8, not yet run")* | **Untested, named in advance:** `enrich_parcels` is ~~2,153~~ **2,068 (corrected 2026-09-10, measured — the same stale figure was carried in Spec 122 §8.2's archetype table, fixed in the same pass)** lines, 5 passes, the largest single conversion in the programme; its clock-relative comps-window gate (Spec 122a's own open question Q3 — "no count- or watermark-based gate can ever skip it") has no analogue in any archetype converted so far, and is the last of the eight-archetype coverage table's rows to be proven against real code rather than the aspirational dispatch-map comment in `scripts/lib/step/index.js`. |

## Appendix §B3 — GAP G-1 closure record (moved from Spec 124 §2 Rule 1) — HISTORICAL, 2026-10-04

Moved from Spec 124 — Dated closure record of GAP G-1 (2026-08-29); Rule 1's text, Enforced-by and Violation lines stay live in Spec 124. Spec 124 §5 needs room for the FLEET-2 register rows (R-BH/R-BI reserved + R-BJ…R-BN, plan fold 18 O-C12(b); about 8.4 KB against 5.5 KB headroom).

- ✅ **GAP G-1 CLOSED (2026-08-29).** The *preference order itself* still cannot be verified after the fact (there is no artifact proving a cheaper rung was tried first) — what closes instead is that reaching for the last-resort rung must now be DECLARED, in writing, at the moment it happens. `scripts/steps/_schema/schema-baseline.json` snapshots every `category.field` pair the schema declares; `scripts/steps/_schema/generate-schema-baseline.mjs --check` (shelled by `step-conformance.infra.test.ts`'s "G-1" lock) requires any field NOT in that snapshot to carry `x-ruling: {rungs_tried, why}` on its own schema node, or fails the build — the same "adjudication is a reviewed diff, not something a descriptor grants itself" posture `grandfathered.json` already uses for V7. `--write` regenerates the baseline, intended to run only after a human reviews the new field's `x-ruling` in the same diff. Proven both directions (RED on a fixture field with no `x-ruling`, GREEN on the same field once one is added).

## Appendix §B4 — GAP G-4 and GAP (R-D) closure records (moved from Spec 124 §2 Rule 3) — HISTORICAL, 2026-10-04

Moved from Spec 124 — Dated closure records of GAP G-4 (2026-08-29 / R-BA gate B 2026-09-26) and GAP (R-D) (2026-09-03); Rule 3's text and Enforced-by stay live; R-BA stays the live row. Spec 124 §5 needs room for the FLEET-2 register rows (R-BH/R-BI reserved + R-BJ…R-BN, plan fold 18 O-C12(b); about 8.4 KB against 5.5 KB headroom).

- ✅ **GAP G-4 CLOSED for the VERDICT half (2026-08-29); WRITE-affecting CLOSED by §5 R-BA gate B (2026-09-26, fast invariant #29).** VERDICT-affecting is mechanically decidable — a `config.logic_variables[]` entry is verdict-affecting iff some `checks[].limit_from_config` in the same descriptor names it, the schema's own established mechanism for "this value moves a check's PASS/WARN/FAIL boundary." `scripts/analysis/step-validate.mjs`'s `checkOnInvalidFail` requires `on_invalid:"fail"` for every such variable, UNLESS `deviations[]` carries a dated, adjudicated exception naming `on_invalid`/`fail` — real descriptors already have one: `load_ravines`'s 6 variables are all `default` despite 3 being verdict-affecting, ratified against the mig-099 cloud-seed-timing hole (`fail` would halt the `sources` chain on every un-seeded database). Measured live 2026-08-29: **0 violations across all 5 converted steps** — every verdict-affecting variable is already correctly `fail`, or covered by a reviewed deviation. WRITE-affecting had no equivalent named schema mechanism (no `*_write_affecting` field convention exists) — gate B closes it stricter: ANY non-`fail` `on_invalid`, verdict- or write-affecting, needs a `deviations[]` entry dated a valid ISO date AND naming that exact variable (`scripts/analysis/gates/on-invalid.mjs`); a blanket deviation naming no variable does not count. 12 measured violations (assert_schema 3, load_ravines 3, link_massing 3, link_parcel_addresses 1, compute_centroids 1, link_parcels 1 — `link_neighbourhoods` already GREEN via its 2026-09-16 named deviation) land as dated `standard-gates-ledger.json` rows per the 2026-09-26 OPERATOR DIRECTION "McDonald's Airtight" scope §3 (DEFERRED), closing trigger "chain_sources conversion complete" (R-AQ).
- ✅ **GAP (R-D) CLOSED (corrected 2026-09-03, WF3 cloud-parity FIX 1.6 — this line was stale).** The chain-start presence check (`declared_logic_variables_present` on `assert_schema`, `severity:"FAIL"`, `blocking:true`, `when:"pre"`) IS in the descriptor: 42 keys in `checks[].expect`, locked equal to `config.probe_presence` and the live `scripts/lib/declared-logic-variables.js` derivation by `src/tests/steps/assert_schema/`. `config.js`'s runtime throw (LM-D15) remains the backstop for steps that run no `assert_schema` at all (entities/wsib/deep_scrapes — a separate, narrower gap, unchanged). See Spec 122 R-D.

## Appendix §B5 — GAP G-2 closure record (moved from Spec 124 §2 Rule 4) — HISTORICAL, 2026-10-04

Moved from Spec 124 — Dated closure record of GAP G-2 (2026-08-29); Rule 4's text and Enforced-by stay live. Spec 124 §5 needs room for the FLEET-2 register rows (R-BH/R-BI reserved + R-BJ…R-BN, plan fold 18 O-C12(b); about 8.4 KB against 5.5 KB headroom).

- ✅ **GAP G-2 CLOSED (2026-08-29), narrowed honestly.** A disposition (a markdown table row in an assessment report) and a specific check's `why` (a JSON string in a descriptor) share no id to join on — there is no mechanical way to prove row N's rule IS check id X's `why` without semantic understanding this tool does not have. What closes instead, matching the report culture's own existing practice (pilot 3's Intent Ledger already writes *"preserved-in-compute (`buildMatchSql`, A-2) ... DECLARED in `notes.json`"*): `scripts/analysis/step-validate.mjs`'s `checkPreservedInComputeHasWhy` requires every `preserved-in-compute` Intent Ledger row to itself NAME where the rule is written down (`why`/`notes.json`/`checks[]` appearing in the same row) — feeds the Rule-4 policy-matrix status. Proven both directions in the tool's own self-test (Spec 121 §12b.6), exercised on every `step:validate` run. **Measured live 2026-09-03 (CC-D4), superseding the 2026-08-29 reading:** 1 of 8 converted steps (`link_wsib` 4/8 rows ungrounded) currently fails this check — `assert_schema` is now green (its 5/6-ungrounded reading was stale; peel 8b's row-derived cascade closed it) and `compute_centroids` was closed this commit (3/3 rows grounded — 2 cited in-row, 1 re-disposed `knowingly-retired`, `docs/reports/2026-08-29-pilot6-compute-centroids-assessment.md` §11). `link_wsib`'s red is left as-is, one finding per WF3, per the same "do not retroactively fix pilots 1-4" discipline `review_followups.md`'s R-R backfill entry already states. **Re-measured live 2026-09-03 (LW-D21, same day, later commit):** `link_wsib` narrowed 4/8 → 2/8 ungrounded — 2 rows cited in place (`30ff8805`, `b71db6e0`; no descriptor/notes.json change), 2 rows (`a81c6a7c`, `76dcca28`) had a REFUTED premise (the construct they describe was never ported into the converted compute — the same supersession class CC-D4 found) and were reported for operator ruling rather than silently re-disposed, per that WF3's explicit instruction. **Ruled the same day, one commit later:** the operator adjudicated both — `a81c6a7c` → `encoded-as-descriptor-field`, `76dcca28` → `knowingly-retired` (mirrors `1933c1e0`'s own Row C precedent). **GAP G-2 is now fully CLOSED across all 8 converted steps** — 0 of 8 steps fail this check as of `link_wsib`'s re-write; see `docs/reports/2026-08-28-pilot4-link-wsib-assessment.md` §12 and `docs/reports/defect-ledger.md` `LW-D21` (CLOSED). **Caveat: this check is textual, not semantic** — `checkPreservedInComputeHasWhy` passes on the literal presence of `why`/`notes.json`/`checks[]` in the row, so a row can read green while its own prose says in words that the grounding is absent (a gap already filed as a followup); the gate is a citation check, never weakened to a semantic one.

## Appendix §B7 — Register rows R-A…R-AI (moved from Spec 124 §5) — LIVE REGISTER, 2026-10-07

Moved from Spec 124 — P1-C9 register move (QUEUE 2026-10-06: one owner, P1-C9; fleet2-assembly/P1-C9.md item 5). Spec 124 had 128 B of arm (iv) headroom and owed two §5 rows (R-BS geom drift Q1/Q2, R-BT Spec 78 Q9). Rows R-A…R-AI (the first contiguous register block, 2026-08-28…09-15) stay in force as Spec 124 §5 rulings and are cited as Spec 124 R-x; spec-split-check arm (iii) resolves them in 124a sections headed LIVE REGISTER. New rows are still added to Spec 124 §5.

| # | Ruling (one line) | Amends | Full text |
|---|---|---|---|
| R-A | Retirement of a tunable is a declaration (`config.retired[]`), never deletion of the live registry row; a retired name still holding a live row is a WARN | Rule 3 · `config` category | Spec 122 R-A |
| R-B | `recovery.interrupted` is a required, truthful declaration on any destructive-retraction write target. **CLOSED for the declaration + before-image (R-M) as of pilot 4** (`d07529af`, LG-17 — before-image live-fired on a genuine forced-FULL) **and CLOSED for the runtime reader as of 2026-08-29** (`LW-D20`/`LG-19` — `staleness.detectInterruptedRetraction` + `selectMode`'s unconditional FULL branch, wired into `runCascadePhase`/`runLinkPhase`; two bugs found and fixed by a live kill-and-rerun proof against `link_wsib` — see Spec 122 R-B and the pilot 4 assessment §R addendum). "Declared" and "automatically enforced" are now the same posture for any step declaring `force_full_on_next_run`. **Recurrence found and closed 2026-09-03**: `runMaterializePhase` (added at pilot 5, `5ee14f5b`) never received this fold — its `bypassed` omitted the `interruptedRetraction.interrupted` term entirely, the same unreachable-behind-the-ledger-gated-skip shape LW-D20 fixed for CASCADE — now wired identically (fake-pool RED→GREEN lock in `step-library.logic.test.ts`; no live MATERIALIZER declares `force_full_on_next_run` today so this closes a latent archetype-level gap, not an observed defect) | Rule 12 | Spec 122 R-B |
| R-C | The golden differential is a mechanical fingerprint lockfile gate, never a commit-message claim. **CLOSED** — `golden-fingerprint.infra.test.ts` was built at `3f7d6645` (pre-dates the R-T addendum programme); this row's own "not yet built" note was stale documentation debt, corrected 2026-08-30 while adding the row below. **Extended, R-T addendum commit 3 (Fold B-10):** the SAME "mechanical, not claimed" principle now also proves an invariant/plausibility MIGRATION preserves VALUES, not just declarations — before a migrated step's golden `invariants.json` is deleted, a round-trip regression lock proves the descriptor-driven executor's computed value is IDENTICAL to the retired file's value for the same `sql`, against real DB state (`link_massing`, commit 3, first migrated; the remaining 4 steps' commit-4 migrations inherit the same executor code path this lock validated, not a per-step re-proof) | §7 Step 4 | Spec 122 R-C; `golden-fingerprint.infra.test.ts` |
| R-D | `assert_schema` gains `declared_logic_variables_present` (blocking, `when:"pre"`) wherever it runs today; ✅ in the descriptor (42 keys, corrected 2026-09-03 — was stale ⚠ GAP text) — LM-D15's throw remains the backstop only where `assert_schema` itself does not run | Rule 3 | Spec 122 R-D |
| R-E | Gate G7's mutation-≥80% clause is replaced by a both-directions red-first lock per class-A behaviour; mutation testing is a bounded followup, not a gate | Spec 123 §6 G7 | Spec 122 R-E |
| R-F | A standing `§R Reflection` section (low-confidence + recurring/standard-shaping tables) is required after every pilot's cutover starting pilot 4; feeds §4.6's promotion criterion | §4.6 | Spec 122 R-F |
| R-G | PRESENCE of a declared variable's `logic_variables` row is always FAIL (LM-D15, R-D); VALIDITY is per-variable via the closed `on_invalid` enum — `fail` MANDATORY for a verdict-/write-affecting variable, `default`/`clamp` allowed otherwise with a `why` | Rule 3 (rewrites the old P4 sentence) | this spec, §2 Rule 3 |
| R-H | A metric expected to be permanently non-zero is WARN with a declared, machine-observable retighten condition — never FAIL, never INFO-by-taste; INFO only for a purely descriptive counter no threshold could bound, declared in `checks[].why`. | R-K.1 | `pending` entries gain a `stage` field (`"red_suite"` \| `"shape_clean"`), letting a new pilot declare `pending` contemporaneously with its commit-6 red suite instead of deferring past it — R-R's fast invariant #5 (`it.fails(` needs a declared pending slug) had never been exercised against a genuine pre-descriptor commit-6 file (only backfilled against already-converted pilots), making it and the pre-existing "pending must be shape-clean" test jointly unsatisfiable for any future pilot. A descriptor appearing while stage stays `"red_suite"` is itself RED ("stage not advanced"). `LW-D11`'s harness-fidelity lock amended in the same commit: a per-step test file with no ctx-builder is legal (assert only when a builder exists) — found the same incident, a BACKFILL archetype genuinely has none. **Amended, pilot 9 commit "step-validate honours declared pending.stage for hard-stop" (2026-09-04): stage gates the hard-stop set.** Every pilot through 8 landed descriptor+compute+runner in ONE commit, so a step's OWN `pending` entry never needed to speak to `step-validate.mjs`'s G7 (RED-evidence)/G8 (golden POST captures)/G9 (closing Reflection) gates or Rules 4/11/12 (compute-rule grounding, order-guarantee and crash-posture reachability) — those artifacts either all existed or none did. Pilot 9 (ENRICHER) is the first pilot to SPLIT commit 7 (descriptor before compute before runner), which makes `.husky/pre-commit`'s `step-validate.mjs --staged` hard-stop UNDECIDABLE-not-merely-unmet for a descriptor-only commit: G7/G8/G9 and Rules 4/11/12 read `enforced-red` for the correct reason (their artifact genuinely does not exist yet) but that is indistinguishable, at the hard-stop boolean, from a genuine regression. `stage` is WIDENED to a five-value closed vocabulary — `"red_suite"` (unchanged) \| `"descriptor_only"` \| `"compute_ported"` \| `"runner_wired"` \| `"shape_clean"` (unchanged) — and `scripts/analysis/step-validate.mjs`'s `computeScorecard`/`aggregateHardStop` reads the step's OWN declared `pending[].stage` to EXCLUDE the gates/rules that stage cannot yet decide from `hardStop` (`descriptor_only` excludes G7/G8/G9 + Rules 4/11/12; `compute_ported` excludes G8/G9; `runner_wired` excludes G9) — the excluded row's own score/status is UNCHANGED (still 0/`enforced-red`, never coerced green) and its `detail`/`note` gains a `"— stage-gated (<stage>)"` suffix, so the printed scorecard never reads as a silent pass. A step absent from `pending`, or a `pending` entry with no declared `stage` (or `stage` `"red_suite"`/`"shape_clean"`), gets TODAY'S BEHAVIOUR BYTE FOR BYTE — `stageExclusions()` returns empty sets for all four, so every downstream exclusion check is always-false. An unrecognised `stage` string THROWS (RED the schema) rather than silently defaulting either direction. Both-directions locked in-memory (Spec 121 §12b.6) in `step-validate.mjs`'s own `selfTest()`: a fixture pending entry per partial stage excludes the right rows and does not hard-stop; the SAME reds with no declared stage DOES hard-stop, naming every excluded gate/rule; an unknown stage value throws. **R-K.2 amendment (pilot 9 commit 8 P1, 2026-09-08): a SIXTH stage value, `"shape_clean_pending_recapture"`.** Pilot 9's own commit-8 sequence (Spec 123 §3.1 pin-then-fix, one policy concern per peel) is the first time a step's compute changes across MULTIPLE separate commits (P1-P4) after the file already reached genuine `"shape_clean"` — but the golden POST captures are deliberately recaptured ONCE, in a later commit (P6), rather than once per peel (a ~40-real-minute `--full` run apiece, to explain a diff only the FINAL post-P4 state needs to account for). Unlike the five original values (each excluding gates that are UNDECIDABLE because their artifact does not exist yet), this one excludes G8 ALONE from a gate that IS fully decidable and genuinely red — a declared, bounded deferral (the pending entry's own `reason` text must name the commit that restores plain `"shape_clean"`, `src/tests/step-conformance.infra.test.ts` locks this), not an artifact gap. `conformanceFindings()` stays `[]` throughout — the SAME promise `"shape_clean"` makes — so `src/tests/step-conformance.infra.test.ts`'s own shape-clean check now accepts either value. Both-directions locked the same way as the original five (in-memory `selfTest()` fixtures: G8-alone-red does not hard-stop; G8+G7-both-red still hard-stops on G7, proving the exclusion is G8-only, not "everything late-stage") | R-K (pending mechanics); Rule 13 (`step-validate.mjs` fast invariants); LW-D11 | pilot 6 (`compute_centroids`) commit 6, `tasks/lessons.md` "a same-day gate must be exercised on the path it gates"; pilot 9 (`enrich_parcels`) commit 7b, `.cursor/pilot9_enrich_parcels_active_task.md` Fold H; pilot 9 commit 8 P1 (R-K.2) |

| R-W | **Compute MUST NOT branch on PostGIS availability** — `hasPostGIS`, `pg_extension`, `postgis_version` (identifier, property access, or a string/SQL fragment naming one), or a `try`/`catch` wrapped around a PostGIS-only call, are the same anti-pattern: a second, silently-selected algorithm hidden inside compute. `guards.requires: {kind: "extension", name: "postgis", on_missing: "fail"}` is the only legal form — the step refuses before compute runs. Enforced both directions by `src/tests/write-class-disposition.infra.test.ts`: every live enum value has a row; every `implemented`/`executor_by_runner` row's cited executor function/runner resolves to a real exported `scripts/lib/step/{write,index}.js` symbol (grep-verified, never trusted from the registry's own prose); no live descriptor declares a `banned_for_new`/`retire` class; the registry carries no orphan row. Measured 2026-09-14 (`bash .husky/pre-commit` against one real staged file, `git commit --dry-run` does not run hooks — `git add` one file, run the hook script directly, then `git restore --staged`): a docs-only staged change (`docs/reports/*.md`) = **65.4s, clean (exit 0)** — dominated by the unchanged whole-project `typecheck`/`lint` cost, `vitest related` itself adding ~0s ("No test files found" for a docs-only diff). A `scripts/lib/compute/*.js` staged change on a CONVERTED step (`compute-centroids.js`, one trailing comment line) = **9.6s, HARD STOP** — step-validate's own pre-existing G8 golden-fingerprint-staleness gate (unrelated to this ruling) fires immediately on the content-hash change, before `typecheck`/`lint`/`vitest related` are ever reached; a genuine compute edit legitimately requires a recapture (`capture-step-golden.js --out`) before it can commit, same as before R-AG. Net effect measured against the ~250s/10,437-test baseline: any commit whose staged files don't trip a structural gate now clears pre-commit in well under a third of the prior full-suite time. The pre-commit preamble `scripts/hooks/check-partial-staging.sh` runs first and refuses (1) a staged file that also has unstaged edits and (2) a staged blob over 50 MB (WF2 hygiene H2, 2026-09-27; locked by `src/tests/hook-partial-staging.infra.test.ts`). ## Appendix §B8 — Register rows R-AJ…R-AP (moved from Spec 124 §5) — LIVE REGISTER, 2026-10-13

Moved from Spec 124 — H5 purity-seam landing (QUEUE 2026-10-13): Spec 124 had ~330 B of arm (iv) headroom and owes the S1–S4 register rows R-CD…R-CM (~10 KB). Rows R-AJ…R-AP (the next contiguous block after M18) stay in force as Spec 124 §5 rulings, cited as Spec 124 R-x; spec-split-check arm (iii) resolves them in 124a LIVE REGISTER sections. New rows are still added to Spec 124 §5.

| R-AJ | **R-X, generalized to the `execution.*` DURATION declarations: each of `execution.budget`, `execution.txn_budget`, `execution.statement_timeout` and `execution.step_timeout` is `executed` (a named, grep-verified live executor) or `descriptive` (declared, NOT enforced, and says so) — never a frozen declaration that reads as enforcement and enforces nothing.** R-X's own row records extending its registry to the other `execution.*` postures as "not yet built"; this is that generalization, in its own registry (`scripts/steps/_schema/execution-budget-disposition.json`, closed 2-value menu) rather than in `write-class-disposition.json`, because these are free-form `duration` values, not frozen enum members. **Dispositions as measured 2026-09-15:** `execution.statement_timeout` = **executed** (`scripts/lib/step/index.js`, the post-check ceiling fallback); `execution.step_timeout` = **executed**, but through `scripts/manifest.json`'s `scripts[slug].step_timeout_minutes` (Spec 118 §3 layer 3, `run-chain.js`), NOT through the descriptor — so the registry drift-locks the two numbers for every `wired[]` slug and requires every other step declaring `step_timeout` to appear in `pending[]`, making the 10 still-inert declarations a DECLARED, dated gap instead of an invisible one; `execution.budget` = **descriptive** (zero executors in `scripts/` — it documents the intended wall-clock envelope an operator reads when choosing the manifest ceiling); `execution.txn_budget` = **descriptive** (zero executors — it documents how much of `budget` the step's own transaction is expected to hold open, the vacuum-horizon arithmetic EP-D17 did by hand). **The founding measurement:** cloud chain-sources run 34971921328 (`main@824ef357`, 2026-09-15) died at the GitHub Actions 300-min wall clock inside `enrich_parcels`, which declares `budget:150m`, `txn_budget:130m`, `statement_timeout:75m` and `step_timeout:180m` — three of the four had no executor at all, the fourth (`phases[].timeout_minutes_from_config`) was executed as a per-STATEMENT `SET LOCAL statement_timeout` giving a 5-statement phase a 375-min effective ceiling, and the CI wall clock was the entire live enforcement stack. Closed by EP-PHASE-DEADLINE: a real wall-clock phase deadline (`pg_cancel_backend` on the captured pid, from a second connection, surfacing through the existing 57014 wrapper — cancel, not terminate, per operator ruling), `step_timeout_minutes: 180` wired for `enrich_parcels`, and the resulting ladder (CI 300 > step 180 > phase 75) locked rung-by-rung from each rung's OWN source file. | §5 R-X (this row is its named generalization); Spec 118 §3 (stop-mechanism hierarchy); Spec 48 §3.6 (silent-timeout class); Rule 3 (every bound an admin logic variable) | `scripts/steps/_schema/execution-budget-disposition.json`; `src/tests/execution-budget-disposition.infra.test.ts`; `scripts/lib/step/index.js` `startPhaseDeadline`; `scripts/manifest.json`; `.cursor/wf3_enrich_parcels_pass3_backlog_active_task.md` C2 |

| R-AK | **`runEnrichPhase` becomes a GENERIC ENRICHER runner: the Spec 58 §9/§11 contract read and the Spec 122 §3.0b defer-scope decision move behind an OPTIONAL, STEP-LEVEL `execution.enrich_hooks` declaration (absent ⇒ the runner does not call them at all; declared-but-not-exported ⇒ a NAMED throw citing the field, never a raw `TypeError`), and the heartbeat + lock-timeout intervals come from two new REQUIRED `execution.*_from_config` fields instead of two hardcoded `config.enrich_parcels_*` keys. A non-finite resolution THROWS above the lock (`Number.isFinite`, never `!x`) — a deliberate `0` still disables the ticker. Closes **ER-D1** (Spec 48 §3.6 silence class): a second enrich-shaped step would have run with `last_heartbeat_at` permanently NULL and no `SET LOCAL lock_timeout` at all, with nothing anywhere saying so. The enrich write path also gains two runner-owned `passCtx` seams (`ctx.retract`, `ctx.joinUpdate`) over write.js's EXISTING executors — before-image strictly first and unwrapped (R-M), class-checked, counter-owned by the runner — so `recovery.before_image: "generated"` stops being a no-op on an enrich shape. Ruled at batch-2 Phase 0.10 to unblock I5 `geocode_permits` and the four Phase-2 C5 ENRICHERs; `enrich_parcels` declares its hooks explicitly and its five committed goldens must hash-equal.** Paid as RE-FREEZE #8 (three additive `execution.*` fields carrying `x-ruling`, ENRICHER-profile `required` widened, `phase_runners[runEnrichPhase].phase_order` re-derived) — `generate-template-freeze.mjs --refresh` + Spec 122 §8 amended in the same commit (R-E lock). Locks L1–L7, all RED-first. | Rule 1 (nothing hidden — an accident is not a contract); Rule 3 (every tunable resolved through `ctx.config`, never a hardcoded key); Rule 12 + R-M (before-image on any retraction; a guard that cannot arm must be loud) | Spec 122 §8 RE-FREEZE #8; Spec 122a Appendix §A9; Spec 48 §3.6; `scripts/lib/step/index.js` `runEnrichPhase`; `.cursor/wf2_enrich_runner_generic_active_task.md` |

| R-AL | **A cloud acceptance dispatch is PINNED to a named commit; the commit it grades is never inferred.** `workflow_dispatch` gains an `expected_sha` input, and the FIRST job step asserts (a) the checked-out tree equals `github.sha` and (b) when `expected_sha` is declared, that it MATCHES — failing **before any step opens a ledger row**, so a verdict can never be read off a stale `main`. The unpinned path is not silent: the graded sha is echoed and the run is warned **NOT valid as an R-AB acceptance run**. **Founding measurement (2026-09-15):** `chain-sources.yml` used a bare `actions/checkout@v4` — no `ref:`, no input, `/headSha|expected_sha/` matched **zero times** — while runs 34964116903/34916671708 graded `headSha 17058af7` against local HEAD `824ef357`; EP-D13 was prose only. **NOT ruled here:** the *reapability* half (the concurrency guard runs BEFORE `reconcile`, so a stranded `running` row blocks every dispatch for 12 h, the reaper downstream of the gate it would open) — own WF3, filed HIGH. | R-AB; Spec 115 §3/§4; EP-D13 | `.github/workflows/chain-sources.yml`; `src/tests/chain-sources-workflow.infra.test.ts` (4 arms, RED pre-fix) |

| R-AM | **A register-ruling id cited anywhere in the 119–124 family must resolve to a real §5 row** — `Spec 124 R-xx` is checked exactly like a `§n` anchor, and a knowingly-dangling one is DECLARED in `known_dangling[]`. **Premise correction (measured 2026-09-15, recorded not silently re-scoped):** batch 2 proposed *extending* the census to `R-xx` ids; that mechanism ALREADY SHIPPED (arm (iii), 2,472 citations, 0 dangling). Missing was the POLICY row — the rule lived only as tool behaviour — and a both-directions lock on the ruling arm (the committed test exercised `kind:'section'` only). The class is live — R-PACE-1 was cited as **“Spec 124 R-AA”** (the spec-move rule) in Spec 122 §8.3 and in I2’s commit-9 message. `R-S` stays never-written and is asserted ABSENT — a silent fill is the rot this forbids. | R-AA (arm (iii)’s ruling half, as policy); §4.3 | `spec-split-check.mjs` arm (iii); `src/tests/spec-split.infra.test.ts` |

| R-AN | **Fleet counts are DERIVED from the registries that own them** — `converted.json`, `manifest.scripts`, the census’s `exemptions[]` — **never retyped into a test.** One deliberate literal is allowed only where it IS the content proven (a named edge list) with a both-directions RED; a bare count never is. **Provenance:** Guardian, I3 c9 — `conversion-roadmap.infra`, `step-schema.logic` and `step-seam.logic` pinned `toBe(12)`/`**52**`/`**54**`/`toHaveLength(6)`, hand-edited every cutover (I2 did 10→11 the same way); batch 2 would pay it 13×. **Measured here:** R-AP moved ONE slug and three literals went red arithmetically while the roadmap was correct. Replacements are stronger, not weaker — `step-schema`’s count canary became a TOTALITY assertion (registered descriptors == descriptors on disk minus declared `pending`), red at any fleet size. | Spec 122 §10.3; R-T | the three test files above; `review_followups.md` (Guardian LOW, I3 c9) |

| R-AO | **A step’s archetype is PROVISIONAL until PH-0 re-derives it from the CODE, and the plan’s commit-form declaration is provisional with it.** A census row or plan table may STATE an archetype; neither is the fact. Once converted the provisional value must be reconciled — `step-validate` fast invariant **#25 ARCHETYPE-PARITY** fails a converted slug whose census row reads `UNDECLARED`, disagrees with its own `descriptor.identity.archetype`, has no archetype at all, has no `template-freeze.json` profile, or is simultaneously a declared exemption (R-AP). **Founding measurement:** `assert_engine_health` was censused **ASSERT** and measured **RECORDER** at PH-0 (R-AE) — RECORDER then had one member, R-PACE-1 became ineligible, and I3 **reverted from compressed to the full nine-commit form mid-flight** (`331f97ad`). Batch 2 declares a commit form for 13 steps off that field. **Amended 2026-09-29 (ledger-to-zero R4):** the 8 pre-R-AO pilots, which carried no census row, now carry rows authored BLIND from the legacy script at its pre-cutover SHA (census `legacy_citation`, batch `C1`), 8/8 agreeing with their descriptors; locked by `census-legacy-citation.infra.test.ts`; #25 now compares 23/23. | §2 Rule 7; R-AE; R-AH | `step-validate.mjs` `checkArchetypeParity` (#25; 6 self-test arms, 5 RED) |

| R-AP | **A THIRD exemption class — `runner_owned`: a manifest slug whose JS file EXISTS but whose concern is owned by THE RUNNER is a declared exemption, not a conversion and not a silent drop.** The two existing classes key on the ABSENCE of a JS file; this one does not, and is the only reason permitted to name a real JS file (enforced both directions). **Grounding — Spec 122 owns the archetype question, so this row records only the class:** §4 gives `reconcile` to **THE RUNNER**; §7.3 row 4 reads *“Reconcile the previous run | RUNNER | nothing declared”*; §7.4 A3 places it at the head of `manifest.chains.sources`. RUNNER is a HOME, not a member of `identity.archetype`’s 8-value enum — “nothing is declared per step, and a step cannot opt out” — so no descriptor, no `x-profile`, no compressed form. Distinct from `identity.gate_exempt` (a CONVERTED step’s field). **Applied:** exemptions 2→3; totality `68 = 12 + 0 + 3 + 53`; **C5 = 13 conversions, not 14**; Spec 125 §0 item 3’s `archetypeRegistry` keys on a declared exemption, not `null`. | Spec 122 §4 / §7.3 row 4 / §7.4 A3; Ask A2 | `step-archetype-census.{json,schema.json}`; `generate-conversion-roadmap.mjs`; `conversion-roadmap.infra.test.ts` (4 arms, 3 RED) |

## Appendix §B9 — Register rows R-AQ…R-AW (moved from Spec 124 §5) — LIVE REGISTER, 2026-10-16

Moved from Spec 124 — H6 conformance (QUEUE 2026-10-16): Spec 124 had ~100 B of arm (iv) headroom and owes R-CT/R-CU (conversion traps). Rows R-AQ…R-AW (the next contiguous block after M20) stay in force as Spec 124 §5 rulings, cited as Spec 124 R-x; spec-split-check arm (iii) resolves them in 124a LIVE REGISTER sections. New rows are still added to Spec 124 §5.

| R-AQ | **A batch's cloud acceptance is a PARTIAL chain run over that batch's converted slugs; the FULL chain is proven ONCE at end of conversion; and where the standing instance cannot complete a proof inside its ceilings, the sanctioned remedy is a TEMPORARY COMPUTE RESIZE, never a raised ceiling.** `run-chain.js --only`/`--from` + the `chain-sources.yml` inputs, pinned per R-AL. Per-slug rows from one `chain_run_id` per acceptance run stay mandatory (R-AB's ACC-1 arm unchanged); what is retired is R-AB's implicit whole-chain scope. **Measured cause (2026-09-18):** Supabase Small (2 GB RAM, 512 MB `shared_buffers`, 22 MB/s baseline) reads ~4.7 MB/s sequential against a 4.4 GB `parcels` heap; `enrich_parcels` exceeds its 180-min step ceiling and full chains exceed the 300-min job cap. **A ceiling measures the STEP; an instance measures the HARDWARE — raising the former to absorb the latter destroys the only signal that says the step regressed.** So `enrich_parcels_pass5_timeout_minutes` and `manifest…step_timeout_minutes` stay UNCHANGED. **The resize is an OPERATOR ACTION the orchestrator REQUESTS via a hard-stop prompt, never performs** — dispatch waits for the operator's live-resize confirmation, and the downgrade waits for a second operator confirmation once the proof and its per-slug batch are adjudicated. New rows are still added to Spec 124 §5.

| R-AX | **A frozen `execution.*` POSTURE enum is `executed` only where a named executor or a NAMED DECLARED CHECK consumes it; a value with neither is `retire`d in place, and a step declaring a tolerant posture (`skip`/`quarantine`) must CITE the check that bounds it.** R-X's registry is extended to the posture enums it named as "not yet built", in its own file (`execution-posture-disposition.json`, closed menu `executed`\|`descriptive`\|`retire`) rather than in `write-class-disposition.json`, mirroring R-AJ's split for the duration declarations. **Measured 2026-09-21:** `on_row_error` and `on_row_error_max_pct` had **zero** readers in `scripts/lib/**` and `scripts/analysis/step-validate.mjs` while 18/18 converted steps declared them; `quarantine` had **zero** declarers and no sink (Spec 120 §6's `step_quarantine` unbuilt; migrations 245-247 consumed by unrelated work) — `retire`d in place, never pruned (R-X). **The runner is NOT the enforcer, and that is a ruling, not an omission:** across the ten runner entry points only `runIngestPhase`'s validate phase does per-row work (`index.js:670`), and it already counts and gates; all other per-row work is compute's, so **R-AT**'s "ownership is by USE, never by declared class" makes a `ctx.rowError()` seam a counter the runner does not produce — the same class-gated form R-AT records as tried and REFUTED. The bound is enforced where it already lived: a FAIL-severity declared check (§7 rung (b)), now CITED by the new `execution.on_row_error_why.liveness {kind:"check", ref}` and verified by `step-validate` fast invariant **#27 ROW-ERROR-GATE** (renumbered live from the plan's working #26, which pre-existing WF3 COUNTER-ROOT already occupied — measured 2026-09-21). Rung (c) (`limit_from_config`) holds for both live citations landed by this WF2 (`load_ravines`/`ravine_geometry_skipped_pct`, `compute_parcel_cost_estimates`/`engine_error_count`) — invariant #27 requires only a real FAIL-severity, bound-carrying check, not a config-resolved one, so a future citation whose check has a literal (not config-driven) bound still passes; `enrich_parcels`' own truthing (`fail_fast`→`skip`, citing `opt_config_engine_errors`, which carries a FAIL-severity `limit` but no `limit_from_config` yet — F-4) is PREPARED (descriptor edit, why-text, both register rows written) but its golden recapture is a genuine `--full` 5-pass recompute that did not complete inside this session's time budget (still executing after 25+ minutes, within the plan's own ~40-minute worst-case estimate, §4) — landed separately, own follow-up commit, not abandoned or re-drafted. The two live literal `on_row_error_max_pct` values (`load_ravines` 0.05, `compute_parcel_cost_estimates` 0) each **duplicated** a registered logic variable and are deleted as **Rule 3** violations; the field stays in the schema as `x-banned-for-new`. Separately fixed, **F-1, HIGH:** the `if/then` at `step.schema.json` (`on_row_error`/`on_row_error_max_pct`) used `properties` where its three siblings use `required`, so `skip`/`quarantine` with **no** bound at all validated (AJV-proven live against `compute_centroids` pre-fix, and against the fixed schema post-fix, both directions). Paid as **RE-FREEZE #12** (one additive field carrying `x-ruling`, one conditional rewritten, one `x-banned-for-new` marker) — `--refresh` + Spec 122 §8 amended in the same commit (R-E lock). Locks L1.1-L5.2 (execution-posture-disposition.infra.test.ts) + the invariant's own self-test arms, all RED-first. | §5 R-X (this row is its named generalization for the posture enums); §2 Rule 1 (nothing hidden), Rule 3 (no literal threshold), Rule 4 (the rule is written down as a check's `why`); R-AT (counter ownership) | `scripts/steps/_schema/execution-posture-disposition.json`; `src/tests/execution-posture-disposition.infra.test.ts`; `scripts/analysis/step-validate.mjs` #27; Spec 122 §8 RE-FREEZE #12; Spec 120 §3.2/§4.1a③/§6 |

| R-AY | **A step that produces a PRICED or MODELLED output declares the PRODUCT POPULATION it is for, as a bound on its own producing step, and every pass deriving from the same source honours the same bound.** A population is a declared predicate over registered logic variables (never a literal, never an id accept-list), it carries a reset arm for rows that leave it, and a parcel outside it stores the declared "not modelled" sentinel with a typed reason — never a stale value and never a priced one. An id accept-list that exists to excuse out-of-population outputs is retired by the bound, not curated. **Founding measurement (2026-09-21):** `enrich_parcels` had already ruled 26,203 parcels out of the max-build model (`emit=false`, 6,397 of them `lot_too_large`) while `compute_parcel_cost_estimates` priced reno menus on 18,529 of them — $77,593,312,011.51 of $598.7 B in `cost_gut_total`, including 103 of 103 parcels above $50 M and all 42 ids of `COST_ADDITION_GT50M_LEGIT`. **Landed:** the population bound (Spec 88 §2.1) gained `max_buildable_gfa_sqm IS NOT NULL` (in the max-build model) AND `cur_floor_gfa_sqm <= product_scope_max_existing_gfa_sqm` (new logic variable, default 750, exclusive) alongside the existing `upper(zoning_class) LIKE 'R%'` filter — result: 26,525 menus retracted, $80.43 B, 0 lines > $10 M; `COST_ADDITION_GT50M_LEGIT` (42 accepted ids) retired whole, dissolved by the bound rather than curated down. *Amends:* §2 Rules 1 and 3; §7 Step 2 rung (a). *Locks:* both-directions in `src/tests/steps/compute_parcel_cost_estimates/violations.test.ts` + `logic-var-parity.logic.test.ts`. **§4.6 recurrence:** the same bound must be honoured by slice 1's apportionment and by enrich-permits §4D propagation — neither is built yet; named here so the NEXT pass that derives from this source cannot silently skip the bound the way `compute_parcel_cost_estimates` originally did. | §2 Rule 1, Rule 3; §7 Step 2 rung (a) | Spec 88 §2.1/§2.4/§2.5/§2.8-§2.11; `ffeb703b` (S0.2, bound + retract arm), `2301f4a7` (S0.4, accept-list retired); `src/tests/steps/compute_parcel_cost_estimates/violations.test.ts`; `src/tests/logic-var-parity.logic.test.ts` |
| R-AZ | **A per-invocation ROW CAP (`--limit=N`) is NOT a step-standard override and is RETIRED BY STANDARD at conversion — declared as a `deviations[]` entry (the `compute_parcel_cost_estimates` 2026-09-21 DRY-RUN entry is the template), never carried, never given a schema home.** Adjudicated 2026-09-23 against Rule 1's rung ladder (and Spec 122's `io_budget` precedent — no field is invented before the rungs are exhausted): **(a) `config.logic_variables[]`** (Rule 3 names "limit" a tunable) REJECTED — a cap is a per-invocation debug bound, not a steady-state value; a standing admin row makes every scheduled run silently partial (the `LINK_MASSING_FORCE_FULL`-left-standing class `override_force_full_present` exists to catch) and is a false affordance under R-A. **(a′) `execution.invocation`** REJECTED — a per-chain argv PIN for manifest↔descriptor drift, not a runtime-read value. **Enforced by (2026-10-03, WF3 chain_args, LP-D17):** `scripts/analysis/generate-chain-args.mjs --check`, a pre-commit line after `src-sql-ledger.mjs --check` (position locked GREEN/RED in `src/tests/hooks-composition.infra.test.ts`). For every step in `converted.json`, `manifest.json` `chain_args` is GENERATED from `execution.invocation` (a chain with non-empty `argv` ⇒ a key; `--write` rewrites only the drifted entry); the invocation's chains must equal the manifest chains that list the slug; a declared or manifest-`env` `PIPELINE_CHAIN` must be absent or equal the chain. Fixtures both directions: `src/tests/chain-args-generated.infra.test.ts`. NOT covered: unconverted steps' hand-written `chain_args`. Found by `link_parcels`, whose descriptor `sources.argv ["--full"]` had no manifest counterpart from conversion (b37087f3) until this gate, so run-chain never passed it. **(a″) `override.dry_run`'s argv arm** REJECTED — `^--[a-z][a-z0-9-]*$` is a bare flag, it cannot carry a value. **(a‴) `execution.phases[].scope`** REJECTED — the frozen `full\|incremental\|deferred` enum is WHICH rows are eligible, not how many. **(b)/(c)** inexpressible as a check or a shape rule. **(d) a new `override.row_cap` arm with `x-ruling`** REJECTED on cost vs need, not expressibility: a capped run is a PARTIAL-POPULATION WRITE with no truthful ledger form — `source-version.js#readPriorRunMeta` selects the latest `status='completed'` row with no other filter, so the capped run becomes the next run's drift/trigger baseline and a trend input, and it can never be a G8 golden input; honouring it generically means a seam in the runner-injected `passCtx.stream`, which cannot cap a set-based pass, so honesty would need a per-pass "streams" declaration on top of the arm, plus `records_meta.row_cap`, an audit row, baseline exclusion, a RE-FREEZE entry, baseline regen, validator matrix and locks (~12 artifacts). **Need, measured 2026-09-23:** ONE converted step of 18 ever had it, operator-debug only, never chain-passed (`manifest.json chain_args` carries no `--limit`), and that step already retired it as a declared deviation with Spec 88's as-built §"Every run is a full run (D1)" agreeing; four UNCONVERTED scripts still carry it (`compute-coa-cost-estimates.js`, `compute-cost-estimates.js`, `enrich-web-search.js`, `enrich-wsib.js`) and retire it at conversion under THIS row rather than re-opening the Ask per step. **The standard debug affordances are** `override.dry_run` (the harness end-to-end, zero writes — R-AV's closed half) and the `src/tests/steps/<slug>/` fixture tier (compute on a bounded population); a bounded PRODUCTION run is not something the standard offers. **Consequences:** R-AV's "regains `--dry-run`/`--limit`" clause is struck to `--dry-run` only; `programme-items.json` `B2-DRYRUN-SEAM` narrows and flips BUILT; the five ENRICHERs' `dry_run` declaration revisits stay a separately owed item. **Spec 120 is the programme's precursor, not an owner** (operator, 2026-09-23) — owners are 122 (runner library, schema), 124, 88, 123 PH-5. | §2 Rule 1 rung ladder, Rule 3, Rule 12 / `override`; New rows are still added to Spec 124 §5.

| R-BJ | **Check SQL reads have their own declared home: `checks[].reads` (`{table, columns?}`), outside `inputs.reads` (operator 2026-10-03, plan fold 9 C7-1).** A check's measurement read is not an acquisition edge: it never enters `stepUpstreams` / `derivedReadsSteps`, ordering or gating. A traced read is accounted for only when it is in `inputs.reads` ∪ `checks[].reads`; the check half is witnessed by SCHEMA-HOMES (`checkReadsCoverage`). There is no check-runner exemption in the gate; that would be a Rule 10 BYPASS. (ii) `outputs.invalidates[].by` is REQUIRED and closed: `trigger` \| `set_null_on_change_of` \| `step` \| `pin` \| `full_rescan`, each with its direction field. There is no escape value. `full_rescan` is armed only under R-BM. (iii) `guards.requires` gains kind `trigger`, probed by the runner (`probeRequirement`) and by `cloud-pre-dispatch`. (iv) `deriveMeta` emits `set_null_on_change_of` columns as writes of their table. (v) #44 check (h) (after P1-C9) answers `h:undeclared` / `h:mismatch` / `h:unwitnessed` / `h:unverifiable`; a column the reader writes itself is excluded. (2) Witnessed: a fresh post trace writes the column. If (1) fails, the answer is `FAIL:WITNESS:<slug>:full_rescan:arm:<t>.<c>`. If only (2) fails, the answer is `UNWITNESSED:<slug>:full_rescan:<t>.<c>`. A LINK/MATCHER step that carries such a row may declare `mode_select:"none"` (fold 17 #1: LINK ⇒ `tri_state`, OR `none` with a `full_rescan` row). | Amends: R-BG (#44 answer set); Spec 122 fold-8 LINK mode rule | `scripts/analysis/gates/witness.mjs` `fullRescanItems`; `src/tests/fleet2-full-rescan-evidence.logic.test.ts`; `scripts/analysis/gates/mode-emits-type.mjs` |
| R-BN | **A same-statement write guard is not a chain-order edge (L-A, plan fold 14; operator 2026-10-03, fold 17 #5).** A step's read of column C is left out of `chainOrderViolations` only when all three hold: (i) the step declares C both as a read and as one of its own writes; (ii) its #44 trace reads C at least once; (iii) EVERY traced statement reading C also writes C. Each exclusion prints as `ORDER-GUARD:<chain>:<p>><reader>:<t.c>`. If there is no trace, the read is unwitnessed and the `ORDER:` row stays. A read in any other statement stays an edge; there is no last-writer-wins (`permits.status` stays ordered). This is computed from the trace, never from a list. | R-CQ | **Host capacity on the Mac mini (24 GB, 12 cores; operator 2026-10-15): the heavy lane is retired; three serialisations stay, each for correctness, not memory.** (a) Dev-DB writers run one at a time (captures, cutovers, recapture nights, `step-validate --write`, migrations, chain runs): they share the DB and before-images. (b) Full suites run one at a time per machine: they share the engine ledger dir (measured: a second concurrent suite failed a fence test that passes alone). (c) Landings onto the programme branch run one at a time; commits in separate worktrees run concurrently (Spec 08 §C.6.4). Nothing else waits. Forks: pre-push 8, pre-commit 4 (`related` and J2), never above 10. Memory floor before (a) or (b): `memory_pressure -Q` free ≥ 25 %, else poll ≤ 30 min, then park. Measured: two consecutive 8-fork suites 68 s each, ≥ 62 % free, 0 swapouts (PC at 2 forks: 17.5 min); "≥ 4 GB free" reads ≈ 0.1 GB on macOS by design. | R-BV | `hooks-composition` (8/4, >10 tamper), `step-validate-vitest-fork-cap`, `host-memory.logic` + `ai-env-check` Host memory line |

It is declared ONLY as a presence-checked `guards.requires {kind:"trigger"}` (link_massing form), never as a `by:"trigger"` row: that closed value means a trigger that NULLs a column (`step.schema.json`; body-checked by `probeRequirement`, MQ-D3). The value columns stay `by:"full_rescan"`. Its effect is stated in `limitations[]` and `recovery`. Why: without it, pairs that stopped abutting stay silently until a reset (~0.17 % of parcels change per publish, mig 251). Until the next run a deleted parcel has no pairs; a reader must not read that as "no neighbours" (averaging is `not_evaluated` until Q-B1). Closes when a library keyed-delete arm with an optional lost-link flag lands (QA O-1), after which the fleet form replaces it. | §6.4 invalidators; R-BM | live: `link_parcel_abutments` `guards.requires` probe; `src/tests/migration-255-parcel-abutments.infra.test.ts` (trigger body) |
| R-CS | **A net-new step stays in `pending[]` until a PRE-reference rule for net-new steps exists (operator y 2026-10-16, H6 QA list; records the load_coa_notices posture).** *(Amended by R-DK 2026-10-17: the net-new PRE-reference rule exists; a PRE is still never copied from the POST.)* A step with no legacy script has no PRE capture to take. A new table's migration enables RLS in the same file (Spec 114 §2). A key-batched target whose rows fan out per key (LINK): the declared `execution.batch` keeps the MEASURED worst batch (rows × bound columns) under the limit, the measurement recorded in the owner spec, until the library splits a batch by `maxRowsPerInsert` (the `executeWrite` form; V3 lane C2). ## Appendix §B14 — V3 admission tables for R-EQ, R-ER, R-ES and the drafts not admitted (w4-rulings-5, 2026-10-19)

Operator instruction (2026-10-19, verbatim): "P4-Swap - as long as this is in compliance with a ruling then proceed or create new ruling. A,B and C as long as they are in compliance and you can show a ruling - if not create a ruling. Everything needs to be standardized as per McDonald's Airtight V3." The operator ruled strict (operator y 2026-10-20). link_coa (`scripts/link-coa.js`) is declared by assert_coa_freshness (`scripts/quality/assert-coa-freshness.descriptor.json`:28) and classify_coa_scope (`scripts/classify-coa-scope.descriptor.json`:22), and is in neither converted[] nor pending[]; `swap-order.logic` already asserts `assert_coa_freshness (runner_wired) ← link_coa: unconverted, not in pending[]`. So assert_coa_freshness and classify_coa_scope, and through them classify_coa_trades and compute_coa_cost_estimates, stay blocked from the swap. A dated note is added to the R-EQ row.

- **PIN LINKCOA-ORDER** (owner: operator / coa chain lane). link_coa runs LAST in `chains.coa` (`scripts/manifest.json`, after compute_coa_cost_estimates) yet writes `coa_applications.last_seen_at` (`scripts/link-coa.js`:239-242, inside its UPDATE), the column assert_coa_freshness measures and classify_coa_scope's cursor reads. Its position (register it as pending, move it, or drop the read) must be resolved before those four steps can swap.

**PINs carried from the drafts.**

- **PIN R-ET-LIVE** (owner: coa lane, first capture). Derived-mode `--plan/--backup/--run/--restore` have not run against a database; the pure helpers and the transactional delete are tested (the delete with a mock client). First live use: `--plan`, then `--backup` on the local stack, reading the transcript before `--run`.
- **PIN R-ET-PERMITS** (owner: permits lane). Permits' key is `(permit_num, revision_num)` and `lead_id` comes from `deriveLeadId` (`scripts/load-permits.js`); neither `--key` nor `--derived-key-prefix` can express it. Smallest extension: `--derived-key-source=<primary column>` plus a composite `--key`.
- **PIN R-ET-COHORT-JSON** (owner: coa lane). `docs/reports/golden/coa/` does not exist yet; its `cohort.json` must declare `{table:"lifecycle_status_history", count_path:"lifecycle_status_history_written"}` before `--run` (R-DN refuses otherwise). The `ROW()::text` identity depends on session DateStyle/TimeZone; a mismatch between the `--backup` and `--run` sessions fails the snapshot precondition (refuse, never guess).
- **PIN SEED-HOLD** (owner: operator, before Night P4). Every plain `npm run migrate` ends with `applyLogicVariables` (`scripts/migrate.js`:330), which inserts every key in `scripts/seeds/logic_variables.json`, including the held 274/275/276 keys. `--only` skips the loader; a plain run defeats the hold.
- **PIN MIGRATE-LIVE**. `--only` has not run against a database; first use `node scripts/migrate.js --only=271,272,278 --dry-run` on the local stack.

## Appendix §B17 — V3 admission tables for R-EY, R-EZ, R-FA, the conflict check, and the bundled RE-FREEZE night (v3-label lane, 2026-10-20)

Operator decisions (2026-10-20, verbatim): "the label V3 should only apply if all the standards are met, compute purity, gates standardized, cross step, registry."; "the label should reflect and enforce the approach" → "y" to enforcement gates 1–3; "y on 2 and 4" → the ninth condition (cited exceptions) and the independent verification rule; "y to all" → (a) fix the G3 scorer header quirk, (b) a closed exception for refresh_snapshot in the hard gate, (c) bundle the two queued RE-FREEZEs into one night. Ids R-EY, R-EZ, R-FA are provisional (R-EW/R-EX are held by the concurrent rulings-7 lane) and are renumbered at integration if taken.

**Conflict check (existing text, cited; none contradicts the three rows).**

| Rule | Where | Quote | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|

**R-EY — the nine-condition V3 label and its enforcement.**

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| STANDARDIZED | One decision (`standardFailures`, `compute-literals.mjs`), one call site (`labelArgs` in `step-validate.mjs`: the printed label and E1/E2/E3 read the same answer), condition (8) is the ③ predicate `checkCutoverPrereqs` itself, condition (9) resolves ids with spec-split-check's one register reader. |
| OBSERVABLE | Every scorecard line prints `standard=V3` or `V2 (fails: …)` with the failing conditions; one `R-EY (9)` line per step names each uncited entry; one summary line prints the V3 count, E1/E2 PASS/FAIL and the converted steps E3 would fail. |
| RECORDED | Spec 124 §5 R-EY, dated 2026-10-20; R-DT, the standard-versions paragraph and the V3 row amended in the same commit; the ratchet file carries its own rule text. |
| SCALABLE | Any step, no slug list except the closed E3 exception (one entry, frozen, locked verbatim, self-expiring at permits ③); the ratchet grows by generator only. |
| ACCURATE | Red-first: the R-EY label cases (14 assertions) fail on the parent and the E1/E2/E3 and R-EZ suites cannot load there (`docs/reports/red-evidence/v3-label/r-ey-r-ez-parent-red.json`); all 39 pass after (`docs/reports/red-evidence/v3-label/r-ey-r-ez-r-fa-green.json`); measured fleet-wide on `--all --fast` (exit 0; 1/43 V3). |
| SIMPLE | Nine named conditions in a fixed order, numbered by position; E1/E2/E3 are three pure functions over the same label map. |
| EXTERNALIZED | The E3 switch is the pinned gate variable `gate_v3_hard_gate_enabled` (seed + migration 279, R-CV form); no numeric literal in the gate code (estate arm 0 findings). |

**R-EZ — independent verification before integration.**

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| STANDARDIZED | One report path per branch (`docs/reports/verification/<branch>.md`), one hook in the existing commit-msg chain beside `check-spec-diff.mjs`. |
| OBSERVABLE | The commit-msg hook prints RED and refuses an integrate-N commit that does not cite a report in the index. |
| RECORDED | Spec 124 §5 R-EZ, dated 2026-10-20; each verification report is itself committed. |
| SCALABLE | Any branch named `…integrate-<N>`; no list of lanes. |
| ACCURATE | `integrate-verification.logic` drives both directions (uncited RED, cited-but-absent RED, cited-and-present GREEN, non-integrate branch untouched). The checks the verifier runs are the ones lanes report: rule quotes, red-on-parent, test counts, exit codes. |
| SIMPLE | A hook checks only the citation; the verification work stays procedural (no library RE-FREEZE). |
| EXTERNALIZED | No threshold: an exact path match and an index lookup. |

**R-FA — header and separator rows are never data rows.**

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| STANDARDIZED | One reader, `tableDataRows` (`scripts/analysis/gates/markdown-table.mjs`), for G3, G-2, both churn-table parsers, G6's ledger reader and #36. |
| OBSERVABLE | The G3 detail prints `table rows=<data rows>` (amended 2026-10-20 by R-FB: `ledger rows=<n> vocab-hit rows=<m>; evidence rows=<k> (not scored)`, §B19); the G-2 detail its preserved-in-compute count. |
| RECORDED | Spec 124 §5 R-FA, dated 2026-10-20, with the measured fleet change. |
| SCALABLE | Any markdown table, any number per section; alignment colons handled. |
| ACCURATE | Red-first evidence `docs/reports/red-evidence/v3-label/r-fa-table-header-rows.json` (4 of 5 failed on the unfixed scorers); fleet change measured: 21 steps rise, none drops. |
| SIMPLE | One rule: the line above a separator is the header. |
| EXTERNALIZED | No threshold. |

## Appendix §B18 — Register rows R-DF…R-DT (moved from Spec 124 §5) — LIVE REGISTER, 2026-10-20

Moved from Spec 124 — v3-label lane (operator 2026-10-20: R-EY, R-EZ, R-FA, McDonald's Airtight V3): Spec 124 was at 379/380 lines and owes R-EY…R-FA. Rows R-DF…R-DT stay in force as Spec 124 §5 rulings, cited as Spec 124 R-x; spec-split-check arm (iii) and spec124-register-locks.logic read them in 124a LIVE REGISTER sections.

| R-DF | **McDonald's Airtight V3 is the final step standard (operator 2026-10-17).** V3 = V2 (registry and cross-step truth, R-BF) + compute purity (R-BZ, R-CA) + EXTERNALIZED (below; operator 2026-10-18: a V3 rule for every future step conversion) + the latent-compliance rulings R-CV…R-DT; there is no V4, and every future step and ruling is V3. A ruling is admitted only when its row answers the operator's seven questions: STANDARDIZED (one form: the majority form or a cited precedent), OBSERVABLE (a `step-validate` PASS/FAIL line or a lock names it), RECORDED (this register row, dated, with the operator's decision), SCALABLE (it applies by archetype to every step; any exception is a closed grandfather list), ACCURATE (measured, file:line evidence, red-first lock), SIMPLE (the smallest change; anything wider is a PIN), EXTERNALIZED (operator 2026-10-17: every threshold, window, timeout, limit, rate or count anything in the estate uses — step, gate, admin surface or tooling — is a registered logic variable (Rule 3, R-CV form, seeded by migration) or an exact zero-tolerance answer; a gate, badge or report that shows a step's bound reads that step's own variable, never a copied number; enforced for compute by gate E #32 and for admin badges by R-DL). Every row from R-CX on carries this label and names an existing lock file in backticks. | Amends R-BA, R-BF, R-BZ; the standard-versions table | `spec124-register-locks.logic` |
| R-DG | **#44 accounting rulings (McDonald's Airtight V3, operator y 2026-10-17; compliance proof `.cursor/v3-r-dg-compliance-2026-10-17.md`).** (G3) A column a reader WRITES itself derives no producer edge in the #44 (e) derivation (`derivedReadsSteps`; extends R-BL(v)); read-only columns, pin edges and `stepUpstreams` are unchanged. (G7) A traced read whose fingerprint equals the step's own `plausibility[]`/`invariants[]` SQL is accounted against that SQL in (a)'s traced ⊆ declared half, at column level; it never enters `inputs.reads` or the (e) derivation (amends R-BJ; no schema field). (G8) A read that only feeds a §9 emit is a measurement read, declared in `checks[].reads` on the emit's reporting check, never in `inputs.reads`. (G5) HELD: a system relation stays unwitnessed until the witness catalog captures its columns (column level, no table-level allowance). (A5) A read newly visible under G9 on a table with no converted inserter raises gate D's UNPRODUCED-UNWITNESSED ceiling by its measured count only (+1 `permit_inspections.created_at`, Python inserter; Claude ruling, for operator review). | Amends R-BJ, R-BL(v) | `step-upstreams.logic`; `witness-w44.logic` |
| R-DH | **A test that needs Postgres is `*.db.test.ts` (McDonald's Airtight V3, B5, 2026-10-17; Claude ruling under the operator's standing direction, for operator review).** It lives under `src/tests/db/`, or beside its step under `src/tests/steps/<slug>/` where a step lock requires it there. `npm run test` excludes `**/*.db.test.ts` and `npm run test:db` runs `.db.test.ts`; never a per-file package.json list; a test calling `getTestPool(` is a `*.db.test.ts`. | Amends R-AG; 00_engineering_standards §5.5; 122a §A27 trap 13 | `step-conformance.infra` (B5 cases) |
| R-DI | **LDG-10 is proven from witnessed traces, never from re-emitted declarations (McDonald's Airtight V3, operator y 2026-10-17).** Fast invariant #50 prints the sources conjunction: (1) #44 hard for every converted slug (P1-C9, folded; `witness.mjs` hardStop covers `converted`; compute-attributed FAILs hard too, R-BZ) — LDG-4 and its KNOWN_GAPS ratchet are deleted, (e) on the effective ledger carries the lock, LDG-D1–D3 close; (2) gate D #31 also counts every unconverted step's observed read of a step-written table's unwritten column (`unproducedStepReads`, MQ-A5 split); (3) #44 (h) witnesses and verifies EVALUATED pins (`op`/`equals`/`bound`): `h:unwitnessed` / `h:mismatch` / `h:unverifiable`, and (amended 2026-10-17, operator y, narrow) `h:undeclared` — a non-ASSERT/RECORDER acquisition read of another step's `source:"run_at"` watermark column with no pin on that writer (a self-written column is excluded, R-BL(v); an ASSERT/RECORDER coverage read is a measurement read, R-BJ); (5) derived order (L-A), the sources adjacency pins deleted; a DECLARED `pg_*` relation joins the witness catalog fleet-wide and resolves at column level (G5). Every answer is exact zero-tolerance; no allowlist, no literal. (4) #44 (i), R-DO. PENDING (printed, never PASS): the EP-PIN-D17 cloud proof. | Amends R-BC (c), R-BF, R-BL (v), R-BP, R-BZ; LDG-10 / P1-C9 programme text | `ldg10-gate.logic`; `witness-h.logic`; `sql-witness-gate.logic`; `witness-i.logic` |
| R-DJ | **Chain tooling integrated (McDonald's Airtight V3, operator y 2026-10-17; CHAIN-TOOLING, R-BC (e)).** (1) Every chain-era generator `--check` runs at pre-commit, and a commit touching any registry input (`REGISTRY_INPUT_PREFIXES` in `step-validate.mjs`, a closed list) validates the whole registry, not just the staged steps. (2) Pre-commit stays file-only (no DB URL, dotenv preload, psql, curl, test:db or cloud:pre) within a budget measured on the Mac mini per command: docs-only ≤ 25.3 s, registry or step ≤ 27.5 s; the budget is a measured target, not yet registered or observed — PIN EXT-1 (runner lane). (3) `scripts/ai-env-check.mjs` prints one R-BC (a)–(e) line per chain from the registries (`gates/chain-status.mjs`), including a report-only count of converted steps the admin surface cannot verify (no `STEP_DESCRIPTIONS` or `STEP_EXPECTED_RANGES` entry).
