| R-DK | **A net-new step has no PRE; its G8 reference is its own committed POST (McDonald's Airtight V3; operator y 2026-10-17; amends R-CS).** A census row with `legacy: "none"` (closed field) is net-new only with the full evidence set: no git history of the script before its descriptor, no legacy_citation, a recapture.json entry with via bracket|env (R-CZ, #38), a red-evidence artifact (gate K), and a rerun-zero proof on every POST; otherwise #22 refuses it by name. For an accepted slug #22 and G8's PRE half print `net-new (R-DK)`; any PRE file on it is RED (no PRE copied from the POST). G8 stability compares each retaken POST with the committed POST of the same file (`git show HEAD:`), diffs explained only in explained-diffs.json (R-DA). | Amends R-CS; R-BA gate G (#22, G8) | `step-validate-net-new.logic` |
| R-DL | **A converted or pending step is admin-verifiable, derived from its descriptor (McDonald's Airtight V3, operator y 2026-10-17: "this is how we verify the output").** For every slug in converted.json (converted + pending): a PIPELINE_REGISTRY and a STEP_DESCRIPTIONS entry; PIPELINE_TABLE_MAP names one of its declared outputs.writes tables (an ASSERT with outputs "none" maps to pipeline_runs, its audit home); manifest telemetry_tables contains every declared write table. The expected set is derived from the descriptors, never a hand list, no grandfathering; a missing or wrong entry fails naming slug and datum. Range badges for converted steps derive from their declared check bounds via audit_table, never hand-written (built by R-DW). *(Amended 2026-10-18, operator y: the lock also runs at pre-commit and pre-push as `step-validate` fast invariant #51, `--staged --fast` and `--all --fast`. It lives in `scripts/analysis/gates/admin-registration.mjs`, file-only, and the suite imports the same pure functions. R-DV holds: a no-descriptor pending row is checked on its slug arms only. R-DU holds: that row never gates, so its #51 FAIL prints as informational.)* | Rule 13 (observability); R-BF; R-DF EXTERNALIZED | `admin-step-registration.logic` |
| R-DM | **A derived secondary write may be non-fatal: `outputs.writes[].on_error: "warn_row"` (McDonald's Airtight V3, operator y 2026-10-17; row text operator y 2026-10-18, runner-lane ruling 1; RE-FREEZE #37).** `on_error` is `"fail_step"` (default; absent = today) or `"warn_row"`. `"warn_row"` is legal only on a DERIVED target (R-DY) written inside the primary transaction and requires `on_error_why` (the R-AX sibling form) whose liveness cites a declared check `{kind:"check", ref}` of severity WARN bounded at exact zero (`viol == 0`) that reads `written.e<i>.errors`; validate.js refuses it on writes[0], without the check, or citing any other check. The runner wraps each slice's derived statement in `SAVEPOINT sp_e<i>`; on error it runs `ROLLBACK TO SAVEPOINT`, counts `written.e<i>.errors`, keeps the primary and continues. The WARN row is the declared check's, never an ad-hoc runner row. A failed `ROLLBACK TO SAVEPOINT` rethrows: the transaction is already aborted, so legacy's swallow (`load-permits.js:484-489`) could never commit the primary either; not a G13 swallow. The witness treats `ROLLBACK TO SAVEPOINT` as inside the open block (`sql-witness/assemble.cjs`). | Amends R-CD (one reporting form, a declared check); Rule 10; R-BZ G4/G13 | `ingest-on-error-warn-row.logic`; `sql-witness-assemble.logic` |
| R-DN | **A cohort bracket may prove one table's writes through gate G's own per-table `count_path` (McDonald's Airtight V3, operator y 2026-10-17; amends R-CZ).** `ingestor-forced-cohort.js --run --count-path=<emits key>` replaces the step-level records_updated/records_new claim with `countPathDecision` (gates/captures.mjs) over the step's descriptor and the capture just written — the same int emit #38 reads, never a second count source; the heal and restore checks are unchanged, and the flag is refused outside --run, for a dotted key, or when it is not the count_path the slug's cohort.json declares for --table (fail closed; Spec 08 output panel round 1, 2026-10-18). First use: link_wsib entities (is_wsib_registered_corrected, 200). | Amends R-CZ; R-BA gate G(1) | `cohort-count-path.logic` |
| R-DO | **#44 check (i): every invalidation resolves (McDonald's Airtight V3, operator y 2026-10-17; LDG-10 edge class 4; Spec 122 §6.4a).** Declaration-driven over the converted fleet: `i:fill-once:<t>.<c>` — a `write_once_backfill` target fills a column once and no converted descriptor declares an `outputs.invalidates[]` row for it (the §6.4a centroid-gap class); `i:unresolved:pin:<t>.<c>` — a `by:"pin"` invalidator with no reader pinning that step; `i:unresolved:trigger:<t>.<c>` — a `by:"trigger"` invalidator with no `guards.requires` trigger on that table. Exact zero-tolerance, hard for converted slugs with #44 (R-DI); 0 rows measured 2026-10-17. | Amends R-BL (ii)/(v); R-BG | `witness-i.logic` |
| R-DP | **An INSERT-only step proves #38 by delete-and-reinsert (McDonald's Airtight V3, operator y 2026-10-18; amends R-CZ).** `ingestor-forced-cohort.js --op=delete_reinsert` is the same bracket for a step with no column to perturb: the cohort is the first N DISTINCT `--key` values (`--order` must be the key), the before-image holds their FULL rows, `--run` DELETEs them in one transaction that commits only when the DELETE's `RETURNING` (key, column) pair set equals the before-image's (else ROLLBACK), the step must re-insert exactly the deleted (key, column) pair set, and the restore re-inserts only pairs still missing, through an explicit column list read from the before-image. Pair mode refuses (at `--backup` and before the DELETE) a table with an inbound FK, a non-internal DELETE trigger, or a generated/identity column (Spec 08 output panel round 1, 2026-10-18). Local-only, under the same lock, recovery command and exit codes. First use: link_parcel_addresses (200 parcels, 260 rows healed); its env-only recipe wrote 0 on a settled DB. | Amends R-CZ; R-BA gate G(1) | `cohort-delete-reinsert.logic` |
| R-DQ | **`--replay-all` retakes a step's bracket captures after its plain/env captures (McDonald's Airtight V3, operator y 2026-10-18; amends R-CZ ordering only).** A bracket's heal is a real step write, and the R-AS instrument restores only the column it perturbed. So a column the step writes while healing keeps its new value: measured 2026-10-18, link_wsib `matched_at` on 50 rows moved the `wsib_registry` hash between the plain permits and sources POSTs. `replayOrder` (capture-step-golden.js) prints plain/env by filename, then brackets by filename. A PRE still rides immediately before its POST (sameSessionDecision), and the cross-step fleet order (converted, then pending) is unchanged. Restoring every column was rejected: it would undo the step's own legitimate write. | Amends R-CZ; Applies 122a §A27 trap 12 (d) | `replay-order.logic` |
| R-DR | **The §1.2a P4 hidden-variable check counts only a step's own consumption (McDonald's Airtight V3, operator y 2026-10-18).** `config: "none"` is a hidden variable when the step consumes a tunable through its own paths: compute reads, its descriptor's `*_from_config` fields, maintenance/distribution keys derived from its own descriptor or compute, and dispatch/fields closures. A runner-wide read in `scripts/lib/step/index.js` (`ctx.config.<x>` / `configValues.<x>`) counts only when `sharedRunnerVarOwner` names this step. This is the existing dead-declaration-only posture of those paths (LP-D-conformance-gap, EP-D17), applied in both directions. Another slug's read is checked against that owner. An unowned runner-wide read is exempt only on a closed list, `RUNNER_OPT_IN_CEILINGS` (the two post-check ceilings the runner falls back from; they count for steps that declare them); any other unowned one counts as the step's own (fail closed; Spec 08 output panel round 1, 2026-10-18). First case: load_bylaw_map_areas. *(Amended by R-EB at RE-FREEZE #37: the closed list moves into the library as `RUNNER_VARIABLES`; post-check concurrency is resolved for every step and no step declares it, so `step_post_check_statement_timeout_minutes` is the one opt-in ceiling left.)* | Rule 3; 122 §1.2a P4 | `step-conformance.infra` |
| R-DS | **The sources prove-red corpus subtracts only converted files in the sources chain (McDonald's Airtight V3, operator y 2026-10-18).** The step-shape count lock is `28 − |converted ∩ SOURCES_STEP_FILES| − pendingInScope`. A converted step of another chain (load_bylaw_map_areas, bylaw_maps) was never in the corpus, so it does not drive the expected count negative. | Spec 122 §5.2 | `step-conformance.infra` |
| R-DT | **`standard: V3` means the whole package (McDonald's Airtight V3, operator y 2026-10-18).** `standardFor` (`scripts/analysis/gates/compute-literals.mjs`), the one source of `step-validate`'s `standard=` print, returns `V3` only when the step has zero purity rows (R-BZ/R-CA) AND zero `standard-gates-ledger.json` rows of any gate naming it (its slug or its compute module base) AND no hard stop (scorecard hard-stop, #44 hard stop or a #44 compute-attributed FAIL) AND no stage-gated red (amended 2026-10-18, output panel round 2 N1: a pending `stage` that excludes a gate from the hard stop — `STAGE_HARDSTOP_EXCLUSIONS`, e.g. `shape_clean_pending_recapture` excludes G8 — while that gate is actually red: G7/G8 below max, G9 failing, an excluded Rule row unpinned enforced-red, or a capture-derived registry gate #30/#31/#38–#41 failing; excluded alone is not red; `stageGatedReds` → `standardSignals` in `step-validate.mjs`, the call site); otherwise `V2`. A caller that cannot answer `hardStop: false` reads V2 (fail closed). Measured 2026-10-18 before and after: unchanged — refresh_snapshot V2 (its CP-PERMITS purity row), the other 29 converted/pending steps V3; load_coa_notices (`shape_clean_pending_recapture`) stays V3 because its G8 is 3/3 and #30/#31/#38–#40 pass, so its stage hides no red. **Amended 2026-10-20 by R-EY** (operator y 2026-10-20): `standard: V3` now needs all nine R-EY conditions, fail closed; the measurement above is superseded (R-EY measured 1/43 V3). | Amends the standard-versions table; R-BZ (the V2/V3 print); R-DF | `gate-compute-literals.infra`; `step-validate-standard-label.logic` |

## Appendix §B19 — V3 admission table for R-FB, the rulings-8 citations, and the bundled-night additions (rulings-8, 2026-10-20)

Operator decision (2026-10-20, verbatim): "y show which ruling authorixzes this or create aruling for each." Applied to three items: (1) G3 scores intent-ledger tables only — no existing rule authorized it, so R-FB is created (provisional; renumbered at integration if taken); (2) the assert_data_bounds `COST_MAG_ACCEPT` list moves into the descriptor; (3) two Rule 4 gaps ride the bundled RE-FREEZE night (PIN REFREEZE-BUNDLE-1, §B17).

**Item 1 — authority search.** No existing rule says which PH-3 tables G3 scores. R-FA (Spec 124:266) settles header rows only: "A markdown table's header and separator rows are never data rows, for every row-counting scorer". Spec 123:305 sets the G3 criterion ("100% of top-right + fence>0 constructs have a recovered *why* or an explicit `INTENT-UNKNOWN`") without naming tables. Hence R-FB.

**R-FB conflict check (existing text, cited).**

| Rule | Where | Quote | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| R-FA | Spec 124:266 | "One reader, `tableDataRows` … G3 (`scoreG3`), G-2 (`checkPreservedInComputeHasWhy`, Rule 4), the churn-table parser … all read through it" | No conflict: `tableDataRows` is unchanged (the union of `markdownTables` rows) and every non-PH-3 consumer still reads it; G3/G-2 read the split built on the same reader. R-FA's detail form is amended in place ("Amended 2026-10-20 by R-FB"). |
| Spec 123 PH-3 output | Spec 123:62 | "**PH-3** intent ledger — `git log -S` every non-obvious constant \| … \| ledger + evidence \| G3" | Supports R-FB: the deliverable already separates the ledger from its evidence. |
| Spec 123 §6 G3 | Spec 123:305 | "100% of top-right + fence>0 constructs have a recovered *why* or an explicit `INTENT-UNKNOWN`" | No conflict: an evidence row (a census line, a lock-inventory line) is not a construct awaiting a why. PIN G3-INTENT-UNKNOWN (§B17) is unchanged. |
| R-R | 124a:132 | "`scripts/analysis/step-validate.mjs` is the ONE command generating the Spec 123 §6 G0–G9 scorecard" | Same one command; the scorecards are regenerated by it (`--all --write --fast`). |
| Rule 4 / G-2 closure | Spec 124:89; 124a §B5 | "A rule kept in compute rather than declared data must still be written down — as a check's `why`, not a comment."; G-2 requires every preserved-in-compute row to name "why", "notes.json" or "checks[]" | No conflict, and deliberately not narrowed: G-2 reads ledger AND evidence rows, so moving a disposition into a table without a Disposition header cannot hide it from Rule 4 (lock: `g3-ledger-tables.logic`, G-2 case). |
| R-EY (6) | Spec 124:264 | "gates standardized: every G0–G8 at its max and no other scorecard hard-stop reason" | No conflict: R-FB changes what G3 measures, not the label rule; measured, no label changed. |
| Spec 123 ship bar | Spec 123:313 | "Ship at ≥14/17 with G6, G7 and G8 full." | No conflict: no step dropped; 5 rose. |

**R-FB — G3 scores intent-ledger tables only.**

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| STANDARDIZED | One split (`ledgerTableRows` over `markdownTables`, `scripts/analysis/gates/markdown-table.mjs`), one test of a ledger (a header cell naming Disposition), the same reader R-FA standardized; every existing ledger in the fleet already names the column (surveyed 2026-10-20 over 42 assessment reports). |
| OBSERVABLE | The G3 scorecard line prints `ledger rows=<n> vocab-hit rows=<m>; evidence rows=<k> (not scored)`, so an excluded table is visible, never silent; `step-validate`'s selfTest refuses to run if an evidence table is scored. |
| RECORDED | Spec 124 §5 R-FB, dated 2026-10-20, with the operator's words and the measured fleet change; R-FA amended in place. |
| SCALABLE | Any report, any number of tables per PH-3 section; no slug list. |
| ACCURATE | Red-first: 5 of 6 cases fail on the parent `0935a5cb` (`docs/reports/red-evidence/rulings-8/r-fb-g3-ledger-tables-red.json`; the sixth, G-2 unchanged, is a preserved-behaviour lock); 11/11 with `table-header-rows.logic` after (`…-green.json`). Fleet measured: 5 steps rise, none drop, no label changes; G-2 counts identical. |
| SIMPLE | One rule (a Disposition column makes a ledger) and one new detail field; the G3 formula is unchanged. |
| EXTERNALIZED | No threshold: a header-cell match and two counts. |

**Item 2 — authority found (no new rule).** The move is authorized by two existing texts. Spec 124 §2 Rule 1 (Spec 124:32): "Nothing about a step's behaviour may live only in code — it is descriptor data, a declared check, a shape rule, or (last resort) a new schema field." The step's own IL-6 ruling (`docs/reports/2026-09-12-batch1-i2-assert-data-bounds-assessment.md`:315): "`COST_MAG_ACCEPT` (`e99ae61a`) is a **declared `accept_list`** (an audited exception list, not a threshold) — descriptor-declared, not a logic var". R-DF EXTERNALIZED (124a §B18) is consistent: the list is not a threshold, so it stays out of `logic_variables`; the R-AU addendum (Spec 124:78) likewise keeps non-scalar data out of `logic_variables` and declares it in the descriptor. Built: `checks[].expect` (an OPEN object, `step.schema.json` `definitions.check`; no schema change, no RE-FREEZE) carries `accept_list` on `cost_estimate_over_ceiling` and `modeled_gfa_over_ceiling`, written by the descriptor's generator from CHECK_DEFS `acceptList`; the compute binds `declaredAcceptList(ctx.descriptor, <id>)` (fails closed) and no longer imports the constant. Dated notes "operator y 2026-10-20" are on Rule 1's Evidence line and on the IL-6 ruling. Red-first: `assert-data-bounds-accept-list.logic` 3/3 red on `0935a5cb` (`docs/reports/red-evidence/rulings-8/il6-accept-list-red.json`), green after. Recapture (local dev DB, under the DB lock, each POST by its `--replay` command): the five POSTs retaken with this change are IDENTICAL (normalised) to the same five retaken on the same DB with the parent `0935a5cb` code, so the move changes no output; against the committed POSTs they differ only by DB drift since those were taken (the target line's applied-migration count 264 → 268, and one sources row count 525372 → 525371), present identically in the parent-code retake. G8 3/3, #44 PASS, score back to 16/17.

**Item 3 — authority found (no new rule); bundled RE-FREEZE night additions (operator y 2026-10-20).** Not implemented now. Three Rule 4 gaps ride the same night (PIN REFREEZE-BUNDLE-1), not before ((5) added at `wf2/p4-integrate-11`). Governing rule: Spec 124 §2 Rule 4 (Spec 124:89): "A rule kept in compute rather than declared data must still be written down — as a check's `why`, not a comment." (3) neighbourhoods: the U+2019 → `'` apostrophe normalisation is written only in compute comments (`scripts/lib/compute/load-neighbourhoods.js`:143, :335), not in `scripts/load-neighbourhoods.notes.json`; write it there (and/or the governing check's `why`); defect N-D21. (4) assert_global_coverage IL-11/IL-12: the `has_bldg` scoping lives in compute SQL (`scripts/lib/compute/assert-global-coverage.js`:258-265, :287) and its rule is written only as the field label "(residential w/ building)"; declare `has_bldg` as a check `guard` on the IL-11/IL-12 checks, the form the IL-11 ruling asked for, with the rule in each check's `why` (the Rule 4 minimum); defect AGC-D8 (reconciled at `wf2/p4-integrate-11`: rulings-8 first wrote the `why`-only form). (5) neighbourhoods: the five `startsWith('Total - …')` section-anchor prefixes are written only in compute (`scripts/lib/compute/load-neighbourhoods.js`:144 comment, :322, :345, :367, :389, :411); `scripts/load-neighbourhoods.notes.json` has none; write them into `read_this_way[0]`; defect N-D22. All three change a descriptor or notes fingerprint, so they take that night's one recapture. PINs REFREEZE-BUNDLE-1 (3), (4) and (5).

| EXTERNALIZED | No tolerance: every comparison is exact; there is no literal beyond 0. |

PIN R-EW-LOCKS (owner: each step's lane at its next lock touch): the existing compute locks feed a hand-built ctx. Rule R-EW (ii) owes each of them a dispatch-built ctx; load_heritage and load_zoning come first because they share coa's 0x path. The closed list, named at `wf2/p4-integrate-11` (verify F4), is every `converted.json` row at 6d63db74 with a `scripts/lib/compute/` module and a compute lock, coa excepted (its lock is now dispatch-built). It is set by R-EW (ii)'s scope, not by a per-lock audit. Converted (29): assert_schema, load_ravines, link_massing, link_wsib, link_parcel_addresses, compute_centroids, link_parcels, refresh_snapshot, enrich_parcels, assert_global_coverage, assert_data_bounds, assert_engine_health, link_neighbourhoods, geocode_permits, assert_parcel_sanity, enrich_ravines, enrich_heritage, compute_parcel_cost_estimates, address_points, parcels, load_centreline, massing, neighbourhoods, load_wsib, load_heritage, enrich_centreline, load_zoning, load_bylaw_map_areas, link_parcel_abutments. Pending (8): load_coa_notices, assert_coa_freshness, classify_coa_scope, link_coa_to_parcels, enrich_permits, enrich_coa_zoning, classify_coa_trades, compute_coa_cost_estimates. permits and the four batch-5 asserts had no compute module at 6d63db74, so they are outside the list. A slug leaves the list when its compute lock builds ctx through the real dispatch.

## Appendix §B22 — V3 admission tables for R-FC…R-GO, the conflict check, and the condition-9 backfill record (backfill-9, 2026-10-20)

Operator decisions (2026-10-20): "y" to the V3 condition-9 backfill (R-EY (9): every exception entry naming a step cites a register rule id), and consolidation decision 3 (put the W4A rulings under version control, one register row each). The W4A source is the tracked verbatim copy `docs/reports/2026-10-18-v3-runner-standardization-trace.md` of the untracked `.cursor/v3-runner-standardization-trace-2026-10-18.md`. Rows R-FC…R-GO are in §B21 (move M34). No row creates a new rule: each records a decision the operator already made, with its original date and source.

**Backfill record.** 218 entries cited no register id at `1d16f155` (29 converted + 14 pending steps). (a) 35 already governed by an existing row: the 19 `spec_version.not_executed` entries by R-BH (124a:218, "a value nothing checks is labelled declared-only"; registry-truth plan folds 8c/8d item 8), plus 16 entries by R-AR, R-AZ, R-BZ, R-DE, R-EP, R-G, R-I, R-W, R-X. Every entry, with its evidence, rule id and where the citation is applied, is in `docs/reports/2026-10-20-v3-condition9-backfill-citations.json`; the (c) entries carry what was searched. Applied now: `grandfathered.json`, `staleness-disposition.json`, `defect-ledger.md` rows and pending-step descriptors (57 + 19). Not applied now: 102 `deviations[]` entries of converted steps, because a descriptor byte changes `source_fingerprint` (`golden-fingerprint.infra`); they ride the bundled RE-FREEZE night as REFREEZE-BUNDLE-1 item (6) (§B17; (5) on `wf2/v3-backfill-9`), PIN FP-RECAPTURE. The neighbourhoods plan lists NB-D1 but resolved it "without the operator" (`docs/reports/2026-09-25-plan-batch2-p3-8-neighbourhoods.md`:123-124, Fold CF-4 by the Schema-Fidelity/Op-Model seat), and the operator ruled N-D1 RULING-OWED on 2026-10-20 (`docs/reports/2026-10-20-operator-queue-snapshot.md`:1659), the same correction verify-12c made for g1-21. LC-D12 keeps R-FV: its plan lists the single-call validation as declared deviation LC-D12 (`docs/reports/2026-09-24-plan-batch2-p3-2-load-centreline.md`:59, :101).*

## Appendix §B23 — #36 single-letter prefixes (authority cited, no new rule) and the V3 admission table for R-GP (tooling-fix-1, 2026-10-20)

Operator decision (2026-10-20, verbatim): "y to all", on two items: (1) gate #36's defect-id blind spot, (2) MV-DRIFT. R-GP is provisional (the next free id after R-GO) and is renumbered at integration if taken.

**Item 1 — authority found (no new rule).** Spec 124 §5 R-BA gate I (124a:211): "STANDARDIZED (gate A closed check bounds; gate I registry coverage)", every gate a HARD STOP. #36's own contract (`scripts/analysis/step-validate.mjs`:2313-2316): "a KNOWN-DEFECT id is DEFINED by a table row whose FIRST cell is exactly that id … an id defined in >1 row with DISAGREEING status cells is RED". The id is "step prefix + `-D` + ordinal" (`scripts/analysis/gates/registries.mjs`:53), and `defectPrefixFor` (`step-validate.mjs`:931-934) gives a one-word slug a one-letter prefix (massing M, neighbourhoods N, parcels P). `DEFECT_ID_RE`'s `{2,5}` therefore hid 38 ledger rows and 33 report mirrors from the gate; `{1,5}` restores the rule's own intent, a bug fix under R-BA gate I. Widened, #36 read 13 drifted mirrors (M-D1…M-D13): the massing assessment's §5.1 fifth cell was its post-conversion plan, not a status. The ledger copy is true for all 13 (descriptor `deviations[]` M-D1/3/6/7/10/11/12; `limitations[]` M-D2/4/5/8/9/13 with F1/F2/F3 not landed), so the report gained a "Ledger status" column (the load_centreline precedent, `f2bd5bcb`). #36: 497 definitions / 101 mirrors → 568 / 134, 0 disagreements, no allowance. Lock: `gate-registries.infra` T6g–T6i.

**Item 2 — authority search.** No rule says an unchanged `--write` must leave its outputs unchanged. Nearest texts, none deciding it: R-BF, R-R, R-DJ (1) (below). Hence R-GP.

## Appendix §B24 — Register rows R-DU…R-EP (moved from Spec 124 §5) — LIVE REGISTER, 2026-10-20

Moved from Spec 124 — integrate-12 (wf2/p4-integrate-12, 2026-10-20): merging backfill-9 and tooling-fix-1 put Spec 124 at 384/380 lines and 130,429/126,959 B. Rows R-DU, R-DV and R-DX…R-EP (21 contiguous rows; R-DW sits out of order further down and stays) stay in force as Spec 124 §5 rulings, cited as Spec 124 R-x; spec-split-check arm (iii), gates/v3-label.mjs condition (9), rule-map.mjs and spec124-register-locks.logic read them in 124a LIVE REGISTER sections.

| R-DU | **A pending step with no descriptor does not block R-DJ's whole-registry pre-commit selection (McDonald's Airtight V3, operator y 2026-10-18; amends R-DJ).** When a registry input is staged, `stagedRowsFor` (step-validate.mjs) selects every row. A `pending` row whose descriptor file is ABSENT (an early ① at stage red_suite) is selected but not blocking: a registry gate has nothing to read before ② (the rule stated at the overlayRows site). The rule is keyed on the descriptor FILE, never the stage label. Once the descriptor exists, the row blocks again, and a converted row always blocks. Measured: without this, no conversion's commit ① could land after R-DJ (permits ① refused at 69c1a1ef). *(Amended 2026-10-18, operator y: the same rule applies to `--all`/`--step` (pre-push, fleet runs) through `rowBlocks`; the red scorecard still prints, labelled non-blocking.)* | Amends R-DJ; R-K | `step-validate-staged-rdu.logic` |
| R-DV | **The R-DL red_suite carve-out (McDonald's Airtight V3, operator y 2026-10-18; amends R-DL; drafted as R-EB in the runner-lane rulings).** The R-DL lock derives its expected set from converted[] plus every pending entry. For a pending entry whose descriptor FILE is absent (an early ①; R-K.1 forbids the descriptor at red_suite), only the descriptor-derived arms (PIPELINE_TABLE_MAP, telemetry_tables) are skipped: there is nothing for them to read before ②, the R-DU reason. The slug-keyed arms (PIPELINE_REGISTRY, STEP_DESCRIPTIONS) still apply. Keyed on the file, never the stage label, so the carve-out ends at descriptor_only by construction; a converted entry is never carved out. No grandfathering. | Amends R-DL; R-DU; R-K.1 | `admin-step-registration.logic` |
| R-DX | **A `ckan_datastore` primary is a first-class INGESTOR primary in four forms (McDonald's Airtight V3, operator y 2026-10-18, runner-lane ruling 2; draft R-DU; RE-FREEZE #37).** (i) Under `guards.srid: "none"` the CKAN arm keeps every record with `geojson: null`; `null_geometry_count` stays 0 and `rows_parsed = features + bad_key_count`. (ii) One targetless CKAN primary runs through `runIngestPhase`: 0y Y4 and Y-I1's `target` clause (its schema twin) are deleted; B7 still refuses a lone targeted primary. (iii) Two or more primaries naming the SAME target form one union group: each selected member is acquired and gated against its own prior sub-block (the group skips only when every member skips; otherwise a skipped member is re-acquired forced), the records are concatenated with `external_id` handed to `shapeRecord`, deduplicated ONCE and written in ONE write; each member keeps its own truth — its dataset version (on every row it wrote), its header and its `guards.schema_drift` decision against its own baseline (the 0x per-primary form, `acquired.primaries[id]`; #33 per external id) — and the group carries no group-level version or header (null, like `content_hash`; standardization trace 2026-10-18). Members must share format, key property and `on_failure`, else 0x U1; a union refuses `skip_scope: "all_primaries"`. B3 is the union rule; B2/B4 count non-derived writes. (iv) `externals[].modes` ⊆ ["full","incremental"] (absent = every mode); `resolveRunMode` (shared with `runEnrichPhase`) resolves it once; an unselected member is `{selected:false, reason:"mode"}`; a group with zero selected members is 0x U2 (a single targetless primary that declares `modes` is a group of one: stamped when selected, U2 when not); `records_meta.run_mode` is stamped (a `RUNNER_META_KEYS` member), closing R-DB's capture-mode PIN for INGESTORs. | Amends 0y Y4/Y-I1, 0x B3; R-DD (unchanged rule, new caller); R-DB | `ingest-ckan-primary.logic`; `ingest-prereq-0x-runner.logic`; `ingest-prereq-0y-*` |
| R-DY | **An INGESTOR may write DERIVED secondary targets from a runner-owned prior-state read, with a catalog-derived conflict target (McDonald's Airtight V3, operator y 2026-10-18, runner-lane rulings 3, 4, 7, 16; draft R-DV, merges the permits-lane draft; RE-FREEZE #37).** `outputs.writes[i].derive {from, prior_columns, why}`: only on an INGESTOR, only at i ≥ 1, `from` = `writes[0].table`, `prior_columns` ⊆ `inputs.reads.tables[from].columns` (a declared self-read; no producer edge). (a) Per R-CU slice, inside the ONE primary transaction: the prior read (`SELECT <full key>, <prior_columns> FROM <from> JOIN UNNEST(<typed key arrays>)`, key types from the catalog, R-CX (ii); a USER-DEFINED or ARRAY key is refused, never cast to a guess; PIN KEY-TYPE, owner runner lane: the declared single-key `key_sql_type` stays the departure/validator cast until one typing source per target is ruled), the primary upsert, the R-DZ touch, the pure compute export `deriveRows(table, sliceRows, priorByKey, {config, run_at, descriptor, keyOf})` (a new key's prior is undefined), and the derived rows through that target's generated plan. (c) `write_discipline.conflict_index` (guarded_upsert only): before any hook the runner probes `pg_index` (unique, on that table, not partial) and renders `ON CONFLICT (<pg_get_indexdef per key>)`; a missing, non-unique or partial index throws, status failed_missing_precondition. (d) One transaction per target in R-CU slices; no per-batch transaction form (`coa_batch_size` is not seeded). | R-DZ | **`columns[].written: "seen"` — a runner-clock column touched for every fed key (McDonald's Airtight V3, operator y 2026-10-18, runner-lane ruling 5; draft R-DW; RE-FREEZE #37).** The column is bound to `run_at` (never compute: a shaped value for it is refused by name), inserted, set on conflict, EXCLUDED from the guard (also under `all_declared`), and touched per slice after the upsert, in the same transaction: `UPDATE <t> AS t SET <col> = $1 FROM UNNEST(<typed full-key arrays>) AS v(<keys>) WHERE <key match> AND t.<col> IS DISTINCT FROM $1`. | R-EB | **EXTERNALIZED is enforced on the code that runs and judges steps: gate E's estate arm, zero ledger (McDonald's Airtight V3, operator y 2026-10-18; runner-lane draft R-DY; closes EXT-1 parts a and b).** `scripts/analysis/gates/estate-literals.mjs` (rule group `estate-no-numeric-literal`, folded into fast invariant #32) scans `scripts/analysis/gates/**`, and `scripts/lib/step/**` from RE-FREEZE #37. (5) `retract: "departed"` on a LINK target (class B's existing vocabulary, a new caller) is the vanished-lead sweep: ONE statement after the loop, `DELETE … WHERE (<declared scope>) AND <key[0]> <> ALL(<every leading key this run derived>)`, before-imaged, full mode only, nothing on an empty derived set or a dry run; disjoint by construction from every key the run re-derives, which is why it is legal under full_rescan where `retract: "all"` stays refused; unscoped is refused. (a) `execution.materialize_source {table, key, pending {predicate, invalidated_by}, why}`: the compute returns `buildSourceSql(descriptor, config, {mode})` → `{count_sql, count_binds, batch_sql, batch_binds}`; the runner binds the named values in order (⊆ last_key, batch_size, run_at), pages by the source key at `batch_size_from_config` (R-DE (i)) and refuses a batch whose last key does not advance; `mode` is `"full"` under `force_full`. (b) A read table with `role: "lookup"` (`unique_on`, `on_empty` fail\|warn) is read ONCE before the loop; a duplicate key or a `"fail"` empty ends the run failed_missing_precondition (R-CX form) with zero writes; a `"warn"` empty is named in `matched.lookups_empty`. (c) The pure export `materializeRows(sourceRows, {config, run_at, descriptor, lookups})` returns `{rows: {e<i>}, replace_keys: {e<i>}, matched}` (runner `targetKey`, e1 = writes[0]); `matched` is additive (numbers add, objects add per key, arrays concatenate); an optional `finalizeMatched(sum, {config, descriptor, post, count})` derives run-level values. (d) Per batch, ONE transaction, writes[] in order: A guarded_upsert (generated; `guard: "none"` renders no WHERE — still the grandfathered, why'd V7 form); F link_full_retraction with `retract: "replace_keys"` (generated `DELETE … WHERE <key[0]> = ANY($1::<catalog type>[])` plus `starts_with(<col>, $2)` under `key_prefix`, before-imaged; immediately followed by a guarded_upsert on the same table); G set_based_scoped with `write_discipline.rows: "compute"` (generated `UPDATE … FROM UNNEST(<catalog-typed arrays>)` keyed on the key, plus the IS DISTINCT FROM guard; `all_declared` = the non-clock set columns). run_at columns are runner-stamped; column types come from the catalog (R-CX (ii); USER-DEFINED/ARRAY refused, the R-DY form). `write_discipline.key_prefix {column, prefix}` refuses an out-of-family row or replace key by name before the batch transaction. (e) An optional `buildPostSql` runs once after the loop. (f) A write failure is fatal (the batch rolls back whole); `dry_run` reads and computes, issues no DML and no post read. validate.js `materializeRowsFindings` refuses the cross-target shapes by name. Members: classify_coa_trades, compute_coa_cost_estimates (wave 4); compute_cost_estimates, compute_trade_forecasts (wave 3). | Amends LG-18 (a second arm); R-BZ (the form exists to satisfy it); R-CY (iii); R-CU (slicing); R-M; V7 (guard none stays grandfathered per step); R-CX (ii) | `materialize-rows.logic` (T1–T10, T5b/T5c panel round 1); `db/materialize-rows.db` (T11, S5) |
| R-EI | **EFFECTOR: a ninth archetype for a step whose output is an effect outside the database (McDonald's Airtight V3, operator y 2026-10-19, C6 Q1).** Members: `dispatch_notifications` (Expo push) and `backup_db` (off-site dump). Its profile has four gates, defined in Spec 122 §1.10: (1) **switch**, a kill switch or injected transport; (2) **ledger**, an exactly-once ledger so a re-run sends nothing already sent; (3) **captures**, where G8 PRE/POST capture the ledger tables plus the intended effects recorded by the injected transport, and a capture or replay never reaches the real service; (4) **dry run**, giving the same intended-effect list with zero outside calls. It is in the census enum now. It enters `step.schema.json` and `template-freeze.json` only in the RE-FREEZE of its first conversion (wave C6-7), because FREEZE-1 arm D refuses an unproven archetype profile (`generate-template-freeze.mjs:233`). No existing archetype fits: RECORDER records state into a table. | Spec 122 §1.10 (archetype drives required fields); FREEZE-1 arm D; follows R-AO (archetype derived from the code) | `c6-rulings.infra` (R-EI arms); `conversion-roadmap.infra` |
| R-EJ | **Chain observation is a RUNNER concern, so `observe_chain` is a `runner_owned` exemption (McDonald's Airtight V3, operator y 2026-10-19, C6 Q2).** Proven against R-AP's own test, "a manifest slug whose JS file EXISTS but whose concern is owned by THE RUNNER". `scripts/run-chain.js:1353` spawns `observe-chain.js` after every chain, so no step can declare it or opt out of it. It is in no `manifest.chains` list and writes no business table (header :4). The four RUNNER concerns of Spec 122 §1.8 gain a fifth home, post-chain observation, governed by Spec 48 rather than a step descriptor. Applied: exemptions 3 → 5 (with R-EK); C6 28 → 26 files. | R-AP (124a §B8); Spec 122 §1.8 RUNNER concerns; Spec 48 | `c6-rulings.infra` (R-EJ); `conversion-roadmap.infra` |
| R-EK | **A step is retired, not converted, only under a closed predicate (McDonald's Airtight V3, operator y 2026-10-19, C6 Q3).** It is recorded as census exemption reason `retired`. The roadmap generator throws unless every condition holds: the slug has a real JS file; it is in no `manifest.chains` list; `covered_by` names at least one `manifest.scripts` slug and every one declares `supports_full: true`; and `evidence` is non-empty. `covered_by` and `evidence` are refused on any other reason. First row: `reclassify_all` (one-off, `eslint.config.mjs:222`), covered by `classify_scope` + `classify_permits`. (1) Each call's request and response are a committed fixture under `docs/reports/golden/<slug>/recorded/`. (2) A capture or `--replay` reads only that fixture through an injected transport; any live call during a capture is a FAIL. (3) A recording is refreshed only by a named operator run, which is logged in the step's `recapture.json`. Enforcement lands with wave C6-6's ①. | R-CZ (captures reproducible from committed data); Spec 120 §4.1a ② as quoted in Spec 122 §1.5 (an unenumerated external input is a declaration defect) | `c6-rulings.infra` (R-EL: no capture without recorded responses); PIN C6-6-RECORDED (wave C6-6 ① adds the live-call FAIL) |

## Appendix §B26 — Register rows R-EQ…R-FB (moved from Spec 124 §5) — LIVE REGISTER, 2026-10-20

Moved from Spec 124 — layer2-spec (wf2/layer2-spec, 2026-10-20): recording the operator's 2026-10-20 rulings as R-GY and R-HB…R-HK put Spec 124 at 382/380 lines. Rows R-EQ…R-FB (12 contiguous rows) stay in force as Spec 124 §5 rulings, cited as Spec 124 R-x; spec-split-check arm (iii), gates/v3-label.mjs condition (9), rule-map.mjs and spec124-register-locks.logic read them in 124a LIVE REGISTER sections.

| R-EQ | **P4-SWAP: a pending step may swap its frozen shell before ③, after its upstreams (McDonald's Airtight V3, operator y 2026-10-19; codifies W4A-R6's "`runner_wired` at the shell swap before P4").** A step whose ② landed at `compute_ported` moves to `runner_wired` in its swap commit; its POST captures land the same night (G8 is not excluded at `runner_wired`, `stageExclusions`). Fast invariant #54 SWAP-ORDER: at `runner_wired` / `shape_clean*` every declared upstream (`inputs.reads.steps`) is converted, or pending past ② and not awaiting captures (`stageAwaitsCaptures`, R-EF), so nothing swaps before the coa root ②; an unregistered or unknown upstream fails. No exceptions. Converted[] registration and the legacy-name retirement (D2b/D7) stay ③'s (EP-PIN-D17). Readers' owed pins fall due in the swap commit (R-ES). V3 table: 124a §B14. *(Dated note 2026-10-20, Q-R-EQ-1, operator y to strict: "an unregistered or unknown upstream fails. No exceptions." covers link_coa, unregistered and last in `chains.coa`; assert_coa_freshness and classify_coa_scope, thus classify_coa_trades and compute_coa_cost_estimates, stay blocked until its position is resolved. PIN LINKCOA-ORDER, 124a §B15. No new rule.)* | Amends W4A-R6 (ordering); R-EF; R-K | `swap-order.logic`; `docs/reports/red-evidence/w4-rulings-5/r-eq-red.json` |
| R-ER | **#44 (i) resolves a reader-side `by:"pin"` row (McDonald's Airtight V3, operator y 2026-10-19; Draft A).** A `by:"pin"` invalidator resolves when (i) a reader pins the declaring step (R-DO), or (ii) the row is the step's OWN column (it writes it, same table) and its own `staleness.pins[]` names another step whose `stamp` is a column of that table: the §6.3 watermark read from the reader's side, the row claim #54 makes an incremental pass index (the enrich_parcels phases[1] form). Otherwise `i:unresolved:pin`. No schema change; the producer still owes its own row at its ② (W4A-R4 (a)). First member: classify_coa_scope (`scope_classified_at` re-armed by the coa root's `last_seen_at`). Its LG-9-hatch guard draft is not admitted; Rule 9 / V7 applies (124a §B14). | Amends R-DO; follows W4A-R4 (a), claim #54 | `witness-reader-pin.logic`; `witness-i.logic` |
| R-ES | **#44 (h) copy window: a pin on a producer still awaiting captures is owed, not failed (McDonald's Airtight V3, operator y 2026-10-19; Draft C).** While a producer is pending at a stage where `stageAwaitsCaptures` holds (the legacy shell is the live writer, W4A-R6 / R-EF), its `run_at` columns leave the h:undeclared map; a reader's unpinned acquisition read prints `#44 (h) OWED <reader>: h:owed:<writer>.<t>.<c>`, never FAIL. When the producer stops awaiting captures (the R-EQ swap, or ③) it is h:undeclared again, so the reader's pin and recapture land with the swap. One derivation for --all and --staged (`buildWitnessFleet`); #44 (i) inputs unchanged. First case: enrich_parcels on link_coa_to_parcels' `parcel_linked_at`. | Amends R-DI (h:undeclared); follows R-EF | `witness-h-copy-window.logic` |
| R-ET | **A derived target proves #38 by a derived-status bracket (McDonald's Airtight V3, operator y 2026-10-20: "2-5 yes as long as the ruling is recorded"; discharges R-DY (e)'s owed bracket; amends R-CZ, extends R-DN, R-DP).** `ingestor-forced-cohort.js --op=derived_status:<s> --derive-column=<prior col> --derived-table=<t> --derived-key=<col> [--derived-key-prefix=<s>] --derived-natural-key=<c,…>` is the same bracket for an R-DY `outputs.writes[i].derive` target. It is refused before any DB connect when a flag is missing or contradictory, or when a derived flag is given without the op. `--backup` freezes (key, `--column`, `--derive-column`) and a sibling `<derived_table>_cohort_bak_<same stamp>` holding the cohort's derived natural keys, rendered by the server as `ROW(<natural key>)::text`. `--run` re-verifies both before-images against live, then appends `<s>` to the guard column and the prior column in ONE transaction. It requires `--count-path=<derived_table>_written`, a top-level int emit declared in `emits[]` and in the slug's `cohort.json` target for the derived table (the R-DN cross-check runs against the derived table). It passes only when: the emit reads ≥ N; the primary's (column, prior column) equal the before-image; no prior derived row vanished; and EXACTLY ONE derived row was inserted per cohort key (0 is RED). Restore: the primary pair comes back under `IS DISTINCT FROM`; the derived rows the cohort gained (live minus snapshot) are deleted by natural key in one transaction that commits only when the DELETE's RETURNING set equals them, else rolls back and exits 2. Local-only, under R-DP's lock (902002), recovery command and exit codes. Approved: A post witness trace with no golden capture and no recapture.json entry is an orphan; it is deleted by literal path when a recipe-backed trace of the same slug witnesses a superset of its statement fingerprints (proved by the printed subset check), otherwise it gets a recipe and is retaken. First application: link_wsib cohort-entities, whose 39 statement fingerprints equal sources-full-forced-5's. Rule: (a) A committed `docs/reports/witness/<slug>/post/<name>.trace.json` is an orphan when `docs/reports/golden/<slug>/post/<name>.json` does not exist and `docs/reports/golden/<slug>/recapture.json` has no `post/<name>.json` entry; a recapture night cannot retake it (R-CZ), so it goes stale (#44 `FAIL:STALE`, `scripts/analysis/gates/witness.mjs`:13) the first time its step's source moves. (b) It is retired by deleting it by literal path, only after a printed check shows that its set of `statements[].fingerprint` values is a subset of a recipe-backed post trace of the same slug; the check's output is quoted in the commit. (c) If no such superset exists, the orphan gets a `recapture.json` entry and golden capture and is retaken; it is not deleted. (d) #44 is unchanged: a stale trace is still `FAIL:STALE`; this row removes the trace no recapture can refresh, never the finding. Lock: `decision-log.logic` (batch I) fails while any committed post trace lacks a same-name golden capture. First application (night 1): `docs/reports/witness/link_wsib/post/cohort-entities.trace.json` (R-DN's first use, b61efc2b) ⊆ `sources-full-forced-5.trace.json` (39 = 39 fingerprints, equal sets). Operator decision (2026-10-21), transferred by generator, never hand-copied (R-HK): the operator approved the twelve Layer 2 open questions of `docs/reports/2026-10-21-layer2-build-plan.md` §6 on a condition, "I approve the 12 layer questions on the condition - we are being pramtica - ie.  keeping this simple, understandable, accruate, standardized, easy to fix or altar.", committed verbatim as the raw record `docs/reports/decisions/raw/2026-10-21-chat-batch-j/2026-10-21-batch-j.json` and imported with `decision-log.mjs --import --chat` as `2026-10-21-chat-batch-j` (CHAT-36.OQ1…OQ12 → R-IL…R-IW, in order). The condition is applied row by row: each row states the approved recommendation, then the rule in its simplest compliant form, and names every simplification with its reason. Four rows simplify the mechanism and keep the approved outcome: R-IL (a generated input file, no runner read), R-IN (`zone` and `exception` tokens carry the candidate set, no unit sha), R-IO (the §5a string form derived on read, no column or view) and R-IU (the existing replace-keys write, no runner field). **Seven questions (McDonald's Airtight V3).**

| Rows | STANDARDIZED | OBSERVABLE | RECORDED | SCALABLE | ACCURATE | SIMPLE | EXTERNALIZED |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| R-IL | the existing generator `--write`/`--check` form; one assembly function | `table_version` stamped on every row; `--check` at pre-commit; the rebuild lock | `#CHAT-36.OQ1`; this row | one file read once per run | any Layer 1 byte change changes `table_version` | no runner or schema change | Layer 1 stays data; no unit or list in code |
| R-IM | one closure walk, the evaluator's own | I4, I6 | `#CHAT-36.OQ2`; PIN L2-EXC-CLOSURE | per exception id | Layer 2 and the evaluator cannot disagree | one function | closure and authorship are Layer 1 data |
| R-IN | one key function | I12 at ② | `#CHAT-36.OQ3`; Q-L2-1 | rows = distinct signatures | every input the results read is in the key | no unit read or sha | the key is what the evaluator reports it read |
| R-IO | one file of closed tables | the both-way lock | `#CHAT-36.OQ4` | a new code fails the lock until mapped | `applies` images by `value_kind` | bare status + parameter; no view | the mapping is data |
| R-IP | the evaluator's kinds, mapped once | I2 per kind | `#CHAT-36.OQ5` | a new kind fails the lock | nothing relabelled | one list | data in the map |
| R-IQ | one declared order | gated lots by gate in `records_meta`; I4, I5 | `#CHAT-36.OQ6` | a gate joins the list by data | no downstream gate on unreadable input | first gate wins | the order is data |
| R-IR | one catalogue, one collapse rule | I13; collapsed classes in `records_meta` | `#CHAT-36.OQ7`; PIN L2-CLASS-VECTORS | a class joins by a pin change | equal results only | one pure function | the catalogue is vocab data |
| R-IS | one scope file | the lineage lock; I1 | `#CHAT-36.OQ8` | targets join by data | every Layer 3 target resolved or deferred | one file | no target list in code |
| R-IT | two declared columns | I1, I14 | `#CHAT-36.OQ9` | per cell / per lot | Layer 3 never recomputes law | two columns | disclosure codes are vocab |
| R-IU | the rows arm's own write kinds | the runner's `retracted` counter; I11 | `#CHAT-36.OQ10` | one key list per batch | no stale link row survives | no runner change | the population is a logic variable |
| R-IV | one builder | I10; the `label_unparsed` count | `#CHAT-36.OQ11`; PIN L2-EXC-CHAPTER | every residential zone | the exception id matches the shard id, by lock | one function | the chapter map is vocab |
| R-IW | one hash recipe | stamped on every row | `#CHAT-36.OQ12` | any number of files | any byte change changes the version | sha256 of bytes | the file list is declared |
| R-IX | one order, Spec 134 §9's | the plan §9 measurement record | `#CHAT-39.Q-L2-3` | one commit ① | no registration against a moving evaluator | one landing | `resolution.logic` names the files that must not exist yet |

**Questions Q-L2-1…Q-L2-4 (ratified by the operator 2026-10-21, `2026-10-21-chat-batch-k-layer2`; applied in R-IN, R-IS, R-IX, R-IU).**

- **Q-L2-1 (R-IN), ratified, applied.** Under the R-IN recipe `inputs_hash` equals `cell_id`. Recommendation: drop `inputs_hash` from the Spec 134 §3 row shape before ①, so there is one key and nothing to keep in sync.
- **Q-L2-2 (R-IS, R-IW), ratified, applied.** v1 runs without a skip predicate: every run recomputes and the write guard makes an unchanged run write 0 rows. A skip on `table_version`, `evaluator_version` and the scope and catalogue shas needs a runner field (a RE-FREEZE). Recommendation: confirm no skip at v1; add it with the next RE-FREEZE if P2-0's measured run time asks for it.
- **Q-L2-3 (Spec 134 §9), ratified as R-IX.** Spec 134 puts commit ① (census row, `converted.json` pending at `red_suite`, admin entries, the migration, the logic-variable seed, the red suite) after node N4: the evaluator prerequisites (`losers[]`, R-EMIT, R-DET, R-LABEL) and P2-0, the round-0 measurement that fixes the physical table split (134:145, 134:159) and needs the local DB. None of N4 has landed, so this lane registered nothing and wrote no migration; the rows' pure functions, the two seeds and the Layer 1 input file are built ahead of ①, as slice (a) was. Recommendation: keep the order; run P2-0 in a DB window next, then land ① whole.
- **Q-L2-4 (R-IU), ratified: option (a), applied.** The population predicate. R-AY (124a:209) asks for "a declared predicate over registered logic variables"; Spec 134's design default `resolve_bylaws_population_zone_classes` (134:36) is not ruled. Options: (a) that logic variable holding the zone list in `logic_variables.variable_value_json` (migration 097), with a lock keeping it inside the evaluator's `vocab.json` `zone`; (b) the evaluator's own zone vocabulary, already in the R-IL input: one source, but not a logic variable as R-AY words it. Recommendation: (a), R-AY as written; `splitPopulation` takes either as its predicate, unchanged.

**PINs carried.** L2-EXC-CLOSURE (R-IM: `exceptionClosure`, by-law fix lane), L2-CLASS-VECTORS (R-IR (i): `scenario_class_units`, fix lane, M-223(4)), L2-EXC-CHAPTER (R-IV: `vocab.exception_chapter` and R-LABEL), L2-69A-NOTES (the dated notes the plan names under M-231, M-232, M-234 in Spec 69a, for the by-law register's writer), L2-INPUT-REGEN (a commit that changes a file under `scripts/seeds/bylaw/` or `scripts/analysis/bylaw/` also runs `node scripts/generate-bylaw-layer2-input.mjs --write`; the fix lane is told), L2-CLOSURE-ESM (gate E's `compute-closure.mjs` follows `require()` edges only, so when the compute requires `evaluate.mjs` its ESM `import` of `dsl.mjs` is not scanned; owner: the gates lane, before ②).
